# DIVERGENCES.md — wire divergences & unconfirmed inferences

The single registry of (a) **deliberate divergences** from the Anthropic Claude
Managed Agents reference and (b) **inferences** about reference behavior made where
neither the SDK types nor the `ant` CLI can reveal it — each to be confirmed against a
real managed-agents recording. The verifier's wire-compat rung resolves intentional
mismatches against this file; an intentional mismatch missing here is a defect. New
divergences land here in the same PR that introduces them.

Format: **surface** — behavior. *Evidence.* `*Tracked: #N*` names the **open** issue that will settle the entry; a closed issue may appear only as provenance — `(delivered)`, or a trailing `landed for #N` clause (the oldest entries spell it as a standalone *Landed for #N.*) — never as the live tracker. Where that issue is the live tracker of more than one entry — #78, the recording tracker, above all — it carries a parenthetical naming what *this* entry leaves open, because the issue alone cannot. `tools/registrycheck` holds the file to all of this: its shape rules run in `make verify` as that package's own test, and `make registry-check` adds the issue-state rules a credential-free gate cannot. Three sections: architecture/compatibility notes (not mismatches), CONFIRMED deliberate divergences, and INFERRED assumptions (each cross-linked to the issue tracking its confirmation).

## Architecture & compatibility notes (not divergences)

Facts that read like divergences but are not mismatches with the managed-agents wire —
recorded here so the allowlist stays honest. Honesty sometimes means naming a neighbouring
behavior that *is* a mismatch, so that a reviewer does not clear it on this entry's authority;
the mismatch itself belongs to the open bug that owns it, never to this section.

- **Session resource (all session endpoints) — end-user binding** — Sessions carry no user_id field at all. Scoping keys are org/workspace/project (reserved, single-tenant defaults in v1); end-user-to-session ownership is an application-layer concern via session metadata and the audit-only created_by. This is a deliberate architectural divergence (explicitly from adk-go's AppName+UserID pattern; the platform stays user-agnostic). *Evidence: CLAUDE.md 'Non-negotiable design principles' #5; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations" (sessions schema: 'audit-only created_by, no user_id'); CLAUDE.md standing product decisions*
- **BYOC worker (POST …/work/{work_id}/heartbeat) — a stalled run stops beating** — A worker whose run reports no progress for `WORKER_STALL_TIMEOUT` (default 30m) cancels the run and stops heartbeating, so the lease lapses server-side and the control plane re-offers the item; it never force-stops such an item, since another worker may hold it by then (#383). No request or response shape changes — the worker simply stops calling a lane the protocol already handles the absence of, which is exactly what a dead worker's lease expiry is for — so this is client-side behavior, not a wire mismatch. The reference worker has no analogue: the SDK's poller bounds only its Stop call (`stopTimeout`, 10s) and has no stall, deadline or max-runtime bound on the run itself, and the `ant` CLI's only worker-lifetime flag (`--max-idle`) stops the *worker* once its session goes idle on `end_turn`, which says nothing about a run that never returns — so nothing here contradicts observed reference behavior; the platform executor carries the same guard through `queue.KeepLease`, which is entirely internal. *Evidence: stopTimeout is the poller's only run-side bound, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller.go stopTimeout; internal/worker/lease.go (progress, hbExitStalled), internal/queue/keeper.go (stall budget, ErrWorkStalled); docs/plan/33_bounding-a-wedged-work-item.md*
- **GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events/stream — SSE preview delta frames** — Streaming preview deltas use type 'content_delta', never the Messages API's 'content_block_delta'. event_start/event_delta frames are stream-only, never persisted, and reconcile under the buffered event's pre-allocated id. *Evidence: CLAUDE.md 'Wire-compatibility rules'; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (events/preview.go: 'delta type is literally content_delta, NOT the Messages API's content_block_delta')*
- **External tool waits and ordered recovery (#375)** — Custom calls and self-hosted worker-executed built-ins advertise `idle/requires_action` with their tool-use event IDs. Receipt is distinct from processing: a later reply or confirmation can be saved before an earlier call settles, while both blocking IDs remain advertised. Processing follows each thread's generated call order. Cloud execution may run between two custom calls and publish its result before the final custom reply; worker authorization permits early execution, but approval alone does not settle its result wait. Model continuation requires the thread's complete result set. This is a local implementation rule supported by observable behavior, not a claim about the reference service's internals. Platform Web/MCP and inline delegation remain platform-owned. *Evidence: [mixed-tool recordings at 146c57d734e935f7c7f1d681a3ba2612d5c1dd82](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agents-wire-recordings/blob/146c57d734e935f7c7f1d681a3ba2612d5c1dd82/2026-09-19-custom-mixed-tools/FINDINGS.md), [order follow-up at 16d24be5acdb0ee4729b8051b5363ce17991ed06](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agents-wire-recordings/blob/16d24be5acdb0ee4729b8051b5363ce17991ed06/2026-09-19-custom-order-followup/FINDINGS.md): ask-first allow/deny, worker-first, alpha/bash/beta, and reversed custom replies; internal/events/orderedtools.go and the API/worker/executor ordered-flow tests.*
- **session.status_running emission cadence** — Emitted once per idle->running flip, not once per tool result while a turn is in flight. This matches the reference's own reconciliation rule — "a turn opens with a single `session.status_running` event", after which the model-request frames repeat per request — so uninterrupted platform-tool execution produces no status transition per result. An external wait that becomes runnable emits the actual idle-to-running transition described above; a partial reply that remains blocked does not. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/events-and-streaming § "Accumulate and reconcile" (fetched 2026-08-22); docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/brain` + `internal/queue` + state machine — the orchestration loop (slice 5)"*
- **agent_toolset default permission policy — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-08-08, plan 29 slice 1)** — Defaults to always_allow (`toolset.DefaultAgentToolsetPolicy`). This entry was opened because the value rested on the plan alone: "the SDK wire types carry no resolved default to corroborate it, and one SDK create-agent example resolves a kitchen-sink agent to always_ask", flagged for a recording against a real endpoint. The public permission-policies guide states it outright, twice — "Each toolset kind has its own default: the agent toolset defaults to `always_allow`, and MCP toolsets default to `always_ask`" and "`default_config` is optional. If you omit it, the agent toolset is enabled with the default permission policy, `always_allow`" — and public docs outrank the SDK in this repo's resolution order, so the platform's value is the reference's and no recording is needed to settle it. The always_ask example that prompted the doubt is no longer present in the SDK checkout (the checkout carries no `AlwaysAsk` reference outside generated code — the union boilerplate in betaagent.go and betasession.go and two type-index links in api.md, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy, exactly as when checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.61.0 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy; the tool-config union split, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolConfigUnion, multiplies the betaagent.go boilerplate without adding a hand-written use), so what it actually demonstrated cannot be re-read; it is left recorded rather than deleted because the doubt it caused is part of this entry's history. #59, which asked for that recording, is closed as answered by the public guide rather than by an endpoint. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/permission-policies (both sentences, fetched 2026-08-08); internal/toolset/definitions.go (DefaultAgentToolsetPolicy / DefaultMCPToolsetPolicy); docs/history/2026-07.md § "permission policies + the confirmation round-trip (slice 7)" (the original doubt)*
- **Every keyset-paged list — `next_page` is a `k1|…` token, not a `page_…` value** — The reference describes its list cursor as "an opaque pagination cursor (a `page_...` value)"; this platform mints `base64url("k1|<dir>|t|<unixnano>|<id>")` keyset positions instead, and has since the first list shipped (the memory-store list, plan 36 slice 1, is simply the newest to use them). Plan 36 slice 2 adds the grammar's second key kind — `k1|<dir>|m|<base64url(path)>`, for the memories list, which orders by path rather than by time; the inner encoding is what keeps a path's own `|` out of the separator the grammar splits on. Not a mismatch, because the value is opaque on both sides: a client passes back exactly what `next_page` handed it, and the SDK's own pagination carries `next_page` as a plain string it never parses or constructs. What the keyset buys is the property an offset cannot have — a page that neither duplicates nor skips a row when writes land between requests. Only a client that hardcoded the `page_` shape could tell the difference, and neither the SDK nor the CLI is one. **The key's presence was a different matter, and there this platform did mismatch**: the reference's `GET /v1/files` always sends `next_page`, explicitly `null` when no page follows, where this platform omitted the key entirely. **Fixed (#544)**, along with the question that came with it — the omission was the files list's alone, that route carrying its own envelope, while every list on the shared one has emitted the key since it shipped: `nextPage` in internal/api/apitest_test.go fails a list that stops. The files list now mints a `k1|…` token like the rest, so the `page_` mismatch above covers it too. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 386 and 391, 2026-09-03/batch2.json idx 266 and 276 — four terminal pages, five keys each.* *Evidence: PageCursor's `next_page` is a bare string, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — pagination.go PageCursor.NextPage, and the documented "`page_...` value", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreListParams.Page; internal/api/page.go encodeTimeCursor / decodeCursor.*
- **No DELETE on /v1/deployments, mirroring the reference's own absence (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference publishes a deployment.deleted webhook but exposes no delete path: enumerating the spec's paths shows post and get on /v1/deployments, get and post on /v1/deployments/{deployment_id}, and post alone on archive, pause, unpause and run — no delete method anywhere — and BetaDeploymentService has no Delete. Archive is the terminal verb here as it is there, and unarchive exists in neither. *Evidence: the six deployment paths and their methods, one generated service method per path and verb, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.New and BetaDeploymentService.Get and BetaDeploymentService.Update and BetaDeploymentService.List and BetaDeploymentService.Archive and BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Run and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, and no Delete, absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Delete; internal/api/server.go (the seven routes registered, none of them DELETE).*
- **Daylight-saving semantics: a nonexistent wall clock is skipped, a doubled one fires twice (plan 37 slice 1)** — Confirmed against the public docs page, which states both outcomes outright; this entry was opened as an inference because neither machine-readable source carries any DST text — a spec-wide grep for daylight / DST / non-existent / ambiguous returns one unrelated hit, and the two cron schedule schemas say only "Literal wall-clock matching in the configured timezone", which decides neither case. The docs page decides both, and this platform matches it: a 02:30 that does not exist on the spring-forward day is skipped; a 01:30 that occurs twice on the fall-back day fires twice, as two rows with distinct `scheduled_at` instants — which is why that column is `timestamptz` rather than `timestamp`, since a wall-clock column would collide the pair on the occurrence index and swallow the second fire. Not a divergence, then, but a behavior worth keeping registered: the two rules are invisible in every typed source, and the second is the kind a reimplementation silently gets wrong. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-29 — "Wall-clock times that do not exist on a spring-forward day (such as 2 AM) are not triggered. Wall-clock times that occur twice on a fall-back day fire twice."); no DST text on any field of the two cron schedule schemas, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule; internal/cron/cron.go instantsFor and walk, internal/cron/cron_test.go TestDSTSpringForwardSkips / TestDSTFallBackFiresTwice / TestDSTHalfHourZone; internal/store/migrations/0031_deployments.sql (the scheduled_at comment).*
- **A fired session's metadata is empty, not the deployment's — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02 and 2026-09-05, #78) (plan 37 slice 4)** — A deployment fire creates its session with `metadata: {}` whatever the deployment's own bag holds, and so does the reference: two recording waves each read a fired session back, resolved its `deployment_id` to the deployment's create body, and found `{}`. The argument made before either wave stands: CLAUDE.md principle 5 names session metadata as the application layer's ownership hook, so a schedule that wrote into it would overwrite the one seam apps are told to use. That argument was registered jointly with the fired session's title, under one "no source says" hedge, but it never covered the title — which the same probes showed this platform had wrong, and which is now the converged `A fired session is titled with the deployment's name` entry among the divergences. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`deployment.run.session.get`, `deployment.create.cron`) and the 2026-09-05 recording (`rec84.session.get.before-delete`, `rec84.adjacency.two-adjacent`); deployment metadata and session metadata — two separate bags with no stated relationship, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeployment.properties.metadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateSessionParams.properties.metadata; internal/api/deploymentruns.go deploymentSessionIn.*
- **Work API auth — a Bearer key naming the wrong environment is 403 `permission_error` (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — A recording of the reference answered a live environment key used against another environment's `/work/poll` with **403** `{"type":"permission_error","message":"Token not authorized for this environment"}`, and answered an *unknown* environment id byte-identically — so not 404 either. This platform now returns exactly that, from the single `workScope` check all eight work routes share. The recording reached only the poll route, so the uniformity across the other seven is an extrapolation and is registered as its own INFERRED entry rather than claimed here. The entry this replaces asserted **401 `authentication_error`**, argued partly on the claim that "no permission_error type exists in the envelope"; that premise was false of the reference and, since #56/plan 31, of this platform too. The same recording refutes it twice over: the reference also answers an environment key whose OAuth scope does not cover a route with `permission_error` ("OAuth token does not meet scope requirement any_of(…)"), so `permission_error` is not confined to the human lane — the comment on `errTypePermission` said it was, and no longer does. The reversal is recorded rather than deleted because the old status was pinned by two tests, and the second of them was pinning a **property this change gives up**: `TestEnvironmentKeyValueBindsToOneEnvironment` required a wrong-environment refusal to be indistinguishable from an unknown key, so that a refusal never revealed a token was genuine. The reference distinguishes them deliberately — a garbage bearer is 401 `authentication_error` "Invalid bearer token", a live key aimed elsewhere is this 403 — so the property is dropped in favour of matching it. The trade is narrow and worth stating: the reference reveals that a token is genuine but never *which* environment it covers, because a real-but-uncovered id and a nonexistent one answer with the identical message (now pinned verbatim, precisely so that indistinguishability cannot regress unnoticed), and a caller holding a live key already learns it is genuine from the environment it does cover. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.wrong-env.cloud` and the unknown-env probe beside it); internal/api/workapi.go workScope, internal/api/errors.go errTypePermission/errForbidden; internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkPollRequiresEnvironmentKey and TestWorkLifecycleRoutesScopeAndMethod, internal/api/worklist_test.go TestWorkListAuthAndEmpty.*
- **GET /v1/environments/{id}/work/poll — an empty queue really is 200 with a null body (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — Recorded: an empty poll answers 200 with a bare `null`, not 204, which is what this platform already did. The entry this replaces chose `null` for status uniformity and flagged 204 as "equally plausible server-side, to confirm against a recording"; it is confirmed. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.empty.block_ms=500`, `work.poll.after-force-stop`); internal/api/workapi.go pollWork.*
- **BetaSelfHostedWork — unreached lifecycle timestamps render null, and `started_at` marks the item's entry to the queue (confirmed 2026-09-02 and 2026-09-19)** — Recorded: `acknowledged_at`, `stop_requested_at`, `stopped_at` and `latest_heartbeat_at` are `null` before their transitions, as this platform renders them. `started_at` is no transition stamp: the reference sets it on a freshly enqueued item still in `state: "queued"`, about 100–150 ms after `created_at`, re-stamps it when a stopped item re-enters the queue under the same id, and leaves it alone through poll, ack, heartbeat and stop — so it reads "the item is queued", not "a worker has it". This platform stamps it at enqueue too, equal to `created_at` (one statement's `now()`) where the reference's lands a beat later. A re-queue here is a new row (*Work items (poll / get / list / stats) — when one is enqueued*), which carries its own stamp; a reclaim re-stamps the row it returns to the queue (*Work-item identity*). *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.empty`, a queued item carrying `started_at`); the 2026-09-19 self-hosted recordings — 11 first enqueues and 2 same-id re-queues across `2026-09-19-self-hosted-docker`, `-self-hosted-latest-cli`, `-custom-mixed-tools` and `-custom-order-followup` (ant 1.10.0 and 1.34.0), and 37 ack and stop bodies carrying the `started_at` their poll showed; internal/queue/queue.go EnqueueThread and PollOn; internal/api/workapi.go toWire.* *Landed for #542.*
- **POST /v1/agents/{id} — an explicit null `version`, and a field-less update, are both 200 no-ops (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — A recording settled both cases this platform had decided the other way, and the code now matches. (1) `{"version": null}` answers **200** with `version` and `updated_at` unchanged — not the 400 this platform returned, which had been argued from `param.Opt` representing null and omitted as distinct states. What the recording pins is `{"version": null}` *alone*, so what it proves is that the null form is not a 400; reading it as full equivalence with omission — that `{"version": null, "name": "x"}` applies the rename unconditionally rather than 400ing — is ours, and is the more client-visible half of the two. It is a safe inference (no reading of the recorded 200 makes the null-plus-field body a 400) but it is an inference, and `internal/api/agents_test.go` labels the two halves apart for exactly that reason. (2) A body naming no mutating field — `{}` — answers **200** with `version`, `updated_at` and the `/versions` list all unchanged, where this platform used to bump the version and write an `agent_versions` snapshot. A version-only body carrying a matching integer is treated the same way here, by the same reasoning; the recording pins only the null form of it, so that much is ours. The precondition still applies to a no-op that carries one: a stale version-only body is still 409. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`agent.update.version-null`, `agent.update.empty-body`, and `agent.versions.list` taken after both, which grew no snapshot); internal/api/agents.go updateAgent (the no-op short-circuit, placed after the existence/archived/optimistic checks); internal/api/agents_test.go TestAgentUpdateOptimisticVersioning.*
- **Session archive/delete — the running-session refusal, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02) (plan 24 slice 1)** — The reference docs pin the behavior ("A `running` session cannot be archived; to archive one, send a `user.interrupt` event by itself and wait for the session to become `idle`", and the same sentence for delete) but not the refusal's wire shape. Ours is a 400 `invalid_request_error` in the standard envelope, message `session <id> is running; send a user.interrupt event before archiving`/`… before deleting`. **The status is recorded now, and it matches** (2026-09-02): the reference refused both with 400 `invalid_request_error` in the standard envelope, so only the wording is ours — its own sentences are "Session sesn_… cannot be archived while its status is \"running\". Only pending or idle sessions may be archived." and "Cannot delete session while it is running. Send an interrupt event or wait for the session to complete." Ours names the event a client must post (`user.interrupt`) where the reference's refusal says "an interrupt event", which is the more actionable of the two on a wire whose event types are the API, so it is kept. The check runs under the session row lock in the same transaction as the mutation, so a confirmation flipping the session to running cannot slip between the check and the archive/delete; whether the reference closes the same window is unobservable from outside. Statuses other than `running` do not refuse, since the docs name only running; that this admits a session at `rescheduling` or one already `terminated`, to either verb, is an inference recorded in the session-threads entry under INFERRED, beside the thread archive's opposite rule. No gate-token work rides along: `gatetoken.Authenticate` already fails closed on archived sessions. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/session-operations ("Archiving a session", "Deleting a session", fetched 2026-09-16); internal/api/sessions.go (requireNotRunning); internal/api/sessions_test.go TestRunningSessionArchiveAndDeleteRejected* A second recording, 2026-09-03, re-drew that same 400 and that same sentence on `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}` — so the refusal is now twice-observed rather than newly settled — and added the fact the first wave had not: `{"type":"user.interrupt"}` — whose event carries only `{id, type}` — is accepted immediately but **does not preempt an in-flight model request**: the session stayed `running` through thirty seconds of polling and settled at the next work-cycle boundary, so the advice that message gives is advice about the *next* boundary, not an immediate stop. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, the `sweep*` and interrupt probes, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md § "Incidental wire facts".* What remains open is not an inference and never will be: whether the reference closes the same check-to-mutation window under a row lock is invisible from outside, so no recording can settle it and none is waited on. *Landed for #64.*
- **Permission gating — mixed always_ask / always_allow turns, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03)** — A turn calling both an always_ask and an always_allow tool gates the whole turn: even the allow tools wait for confirmation (no partial execution while a confirmation is outstanding). requires_action.event_ids still lists only the ask tools. Filed as a deliberate simplicity/safety decision while reference behavior for mixed turns was unspecified; **it is the reference's behavior too.** A recorded agent with `bash` at `always_ask` and `write` at `always_allow`, asked for both in one response, produced two `agent.tool_use` events in one turn — `bash` stamped `evaluated_permission: "ask"`, `write` stamped `"allow"` — and parked on `session.thread_status_idle` and `session.status_idle`, both carrying `stop_reason.type: "requires_action"` whose `event_ids` names the `bash` call alone. A listing taken thirty-nine seconds into that park holds no `agent.tool_result` at all, so the allow call is not answered while the ask confirmation stands. Both results appear only after the confirmation, `bash` first and `write` 260 ms later — the spacing of sequential execution after release rather than of a result computed during the gate and flushed at resume, though what the wire shows is the absence of the *event*, the file's creation time not being on it. The decision therefore stands as the reason the code reads this way, not as a divergence. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "permission policies + the confirmation round-trip (slice 7)" ('Design decision — a mixed turn gates everything, not just its ask tools'); internal/brain/brain.go (the askIDs gate, which enqueues no tool_exec, and the allow/ask stamp beside it); internal/api/events.go (the confirmation that clears the last ask, then enqueues the exec that drains both); internal/brain/brain_test.go TestMixedPolicyTurnGatesEverythingButNamesOnlyAsk* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `agent.create.mixed-perm`, `session.create.mixed-perm`, `session.events.send.mixed-perm`, `session.events.list.mixed-perm` (the gate snapshot), `session.events.send.tool-confirmation-allow`, `session.events.list.mixed-perm-after-confirm` and `session.events.list.mixed-perm-final`; all three listings are forward-chronological and each is an ordered prefix of the next, so the empty gate window is not an ordering artefact.*
- **A dream's `session_id` and `outputs[]` appear in the same commit as `running` (plan 41 slice 2)** — The guide tells a client to expect a gap: the output store id "appears in the dream's `outputs[]` shortly after the dream starts `running`, once the workflow has cloned the input store", so a `running` dream can briefly report an empty `outputs[]`. This platform opens no such gap. Its start arm clones the store, inserts the transcript file rows, creates the pipeline session and sets `status`, `session_id` and `outputs[]` in one transaction, so a poller sees `pending` with both empty and then `running` with both filled, and never the state between them. That is not a mismatch — a client written for the reference's gap is served correctly by a platform that never opens one, and nothing on the wire says the gap must exist. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (the "shortly after" sentence); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.2 and §8.2 item 4 (the poll that would say whether the reference's gap is observable at all); internal/api StartDreamRunner (the start arm's write transaction)*
- **An archived memory store refuses new content with a 400 and admits erasure — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 1, decision 3)** — The reference says an archived store is "read-only" and that archiving is one-way, and stops there: no status, no error type, no statement of which routes the word covers. A recording answers all three, and this platform matches it. On an archived store `POST /v1/memory_stores/{id}` and the memory create and update routes answer **400** `invalid_request_error` — the vault rule this platform already had, worded `memory store … is archived` where the reference says `cannot modify archived resource: memory store …` — while a memory delete answers **200** `memory_deleted`, and so does the redaction of a version no memory heads: the line runs between new content and erasure, not between reads and writes. The delete drew the 400 here until #685 moved it to the permissive lock redaction already took; it still writes its tombstone version. Retrieve and list keep serving the store, which the reference documents ("Returns the store, including archived stores") and the recording shows, and a second archive returns 200 with the first call's `archived_at` unchanged, as recorded. A head's redaction stays refused on an archived store, a content rule the head-redaction entry records. **One neighbouring half is a mismatch, not a note**: the reference moves `updated_at` to `archived_at` on the first archive, where this platform leaves it alone, reading the spec's definition of the field — when name, description or metadata last changed. That spec-versus-recording question belongs to the open **#685**, and a reviewer must not clear it on this entry's authority. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `store.update.archived.rename`, `mem.create.on-archived-store`, `mem.update.archived-store`, `mem.delete.archived-store`, `memver.redact.archived.non-head`, `store.get.archived`, `store.list.include_archived`, `store.archive`, `store.archive.again` and `store.archive.Notes`); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystore.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStore.ArchivedAt, and the spec's `archived_at` ("Set once and never cleared; archiving is one-way. Archived stores are read-only and cannot be attached to new sessions"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStore.properties.archived_at; internal/api/memories.go lockMemoryStoreForWrite / deleteMemory; internal/api/memorystores.go updateMemoryStore / archiveMemoryStore; internal/api/vaults.go updateVault (the rule this mirrors); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryArchivedStoreRefusesNewContentAdmitsDelete; internal/api/memorystores_test.go TestMemoryStoreCRUD; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 3* *Landed for #685's delete half.*
- **A memory delete's `expected_content_sha256` — a malformed value is a 400, a well-formed mismatch a 409 `memory_precondition_failed_error` — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — Delete's precondition rides the query string instead of a body, and how it fails is stated nowhere — not in the SDK, whose param carries only the doc string "Query parameter for expected_content_sha256", and not in the guides; the reference's own worker treats 409 and 412 as the same outcome when its delete is refused. A recording separates shape from mismatch. Sixty-four zeros, a well-formed digest that does not match, draw **409** `memory_precondition_failed_error` — the update precondition's spec-stated status and type, which this platform already answered. `nothex` draws **400** `invalid_request_error` ("expected_content_sha256: must be 64 characters (and 1 more validation errors)"), where this platform let any storable text fall through to the 409 until #684; it now refuses anything but contentDigest's own shape, 64 lowercase hex characters, before the comparison, so a well-formed digest's 200 or 409 does not move, and only an absent parameter is no precondition at all. Three readings here are ours, since the recording probed one malformed value. That a 64-character value outside lowercase hex is a 400 too: `nothex` is both too short and not hex, and its second validation error is unnamed, so it does not say which alphabet the reference checks. And that a parameter supplied empty is a 400 like any other shape rather than no precondition — the SDK sends one for `param.NewOpt("")`, whose `Valid()` holds for the empty string (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — packages/param/option.go Opt.Valid), and which this platform used to read as no precondition, deleting unconditionally. And that a query string which does not parse (`%zz`, an unescaped `;`) is a 400 too, since Go's query parser drops such a pair and a corrupted precondition would otherwise read as absent. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `mem.delete.malformed-sha` and `mem.delete.wrong-sha`); BetaMemoryStoreMemoryDeleteParams, the query-side `expected_content_sha256` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryDeleteParams.ExpectedContentSha256), and the update precondition's documented 409 (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsPreconditionParam); deleteRemote, whose `case isStatus(err, 409), isStatus(err, 412)` collapses the two (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go SessionMemoryStores.deleteRemote); internal/api/memories.go deleteMemory; internal/memsync/tree.go IsDigest; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryDeletePrecondition* *Landed for #684.*
- **Redacting a memory's current head is a 400 `invalid_request_error`, on an archived store too — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 2, decisions 3 and 6)** — The reference states the rule and not its status: "A version that is the current head of a live memory cannot be redacted." A recording answers **400** `invalid_request_error` ("version is the current content of mem_…; write a new version first") on a live store and on an archived one alike, and this platform answers the same status and type, naming the memory the version still heads. The archived store is where the two differed until #686: this platform read "live" as "not archived" and allowed the head's redaction there, emptying the `memories` row to `""`, on the argument that nothing can supersede a head in a store that takes no new content. The reference keeps the rule unconditional, and so does this platform now. A head's bytes on an archived store are reached instead by deleting the memory, which an archived store admits (the archived-store entry), after which the version heads nothing and redacts like any other — a sequence the recording did not probe on the reference. A second redaction of an already-redacted version returns it unchanged, `redacted_at` included, as the recording shows the reference doing. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `memver.redact.head`, `memver.redact.archived.head`, `memver.redact.non-head`, `memver.redact.non-head.again` and `mem.get.archived-store.after-redact`, the last still serving the head's bytes); platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the head rule, and "Archiving makes a store read-only… Archiving is one-way"); Redact, a bodiless POST, and its params (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionService.Redact and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionRedactParams) and the four nulled fields (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryVersion); internal/api/memoryversions.go redactMemoryVersion; internal/api/memoryversions_test.go TestMemoryVersionRedact / TestMemoryVersionRedactOnAnArchivedStore; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decisions 3 and 6* *Landed for #686.*

## Deliberate divergences (CONFIRMED)

Mismatches with the managed-agents wire taken by choice, each argued. A **converged** entry —
one that opens with its divergence already closed — stays here rather than moving to the notes
above: those notes are for behavior that never was a mismatch, and what once was one is worth
the record. Such an entry says what survives the convergence, as `write / edit built-in tools —
atomicity` does; where nothing survives, the closure is itself the record. An entry here may
also carry a live inference without becoming one — its pointer names the recording that would
settle it, as the `block_ms` entry's does — because the section follows the entry's divergence,
not the loose ends around it.

- **All endpoints — anthropic-version / anthropic-beta headers and ?beta=true** — anthropic-version and anthropic-beta request headers are accepted and ignored; ?beta=true is accepted where the reference honors it. No version negotiation is performed — and with Files and Skills promoted to GA (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.65.0 — client.go Client.Files and Client.Skills) that is a shape difference, not only a routing one — a narrower one than this entry claimed until #651. **Both** file shapes carry `expires_at` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — file.go FileMetadata.ExpiresAt; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata.ExpiresAt); only `scope` separates them, and `BetaFileMetadata` is the shape that has it. This platform renders that full field set on the bare path and the `?beta=true` path alike (plan 36 slice 0) — and the recording says the reference does not gate the `scope` key on the path either: a session-scoped file read on the **bare** path came back carrying `scope`, which is precisely what the old GA-shape reading forbade. That one arm is the whole of the evidence — the `?beta=true` reads in the archive are all of unscoped files, which have no scope to report on either path, so they are consistent with this and prove nothing. What the recorded bytes do settle is that the key follows the file's scope, which is why `scope` carries `omitempty` here and `expires_at` does not. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 396/408 `file.get.session-snapshot-id` (bare path, scoped); all eight recorded file objects spell out `expires_at: null` and the six unscoped ones omit `scope` outright — a recorder that dropped nulls would have taken `expires_at` too, so the absence is the reference's. No recorded list element is scoped, so that surface is covered by `renderFile` being the single renderer rather than by a byte.* The memory-store surface is a second, narrower case of the same rule rather than an exception to it: the reference documents `agent-memory-2026-07-22` as the beta value those endpoints take *instead of* `managed-agents-2026-04-01`, and that sending both returns a 400. This platform returns no such 400 — it ignores the header whatever it carries, on `/v1/memory_stores` exactly as everywhere else (plan 36 slice 1) — so a client sending both values is served rather than refused, and one sending neither is served too. Dreams are a third case of the same shape (plan 41): the dreams guide says the `managed-agents-2026-04-01` header alone "doesn't grant access to dreams", and both the SDK and the `ant` CLI — which drives the same SDK methods — prepend `anthropic-beta: dreaming-2026-04-21` to all five dream calls; this platform serves `/v1/dreams` with that header, without it, and with any other value it carries, because there is no grant here to withhold. **The Skills surface is now recorded on both counts, and it splits them (plan 39).** `?beta=true` selects no shape there: five read pairs are byte-identical across the bare and `?beta=true` paths — the list, the skill object, a version, `latest`, and the `/content` refusal — so ignoring the parameter is exactly what the reference does with it, and the write pairs behave identically too. The dated `anthropic-beta: skills-2025-10-02` header is the opposite: a full pre-migration lane, fields **and** behavior. The skill object returns `display_title`, a bare-string `source` and a numeric `latest_version`; the version object returns `version` and `directory` under an id prefixed `skill_version_`; the `{version}` slot takes the numeric form alone, `Invalid version format: 'skver_…'. Version must be a numeric timestamp (e.g., '1759178010641129').`; and `DELETE /v1/skills/{id}` refuses with `Cannot delete skill with existing versions. Delete all versions first.` where the same verb without the header cascades. This platform serves the GA shape to every caller and ignores that header, so a client still sending `skills-2025-10-02` is served GA here and the beta lane there. That is a divergence from the reference and **not** from this rule — the rule is what produces it, and plan 39 decision 11 argues the dual shape down: selecting a response shape from a request header is the one thing accept-and-ignore forbids, it doubles the wire surface permanently, and the single consumer it would spare is one workshop example a two-line edit also fixes. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [0/1, 16/17, 30/31, 32/33, 43/44] (the byte-identical `?beta=true` pairs), [18, 19] (the dated skill object), [142] (the dated version object and its `skill_version_` id), [36, 37] (the dated `{version}` slot), [79] vs [87] (cascade vs refusal); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decisions 1 and 11; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — file.go FileMetadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata, where the split was real; the same structs, where only `scope` separates them, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — file.go FileMetadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata; the header every store method prepends, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreService.New; platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the two beta values and the 400 for both); the `dreaming-2026-04-21` header prepended to all five dream calls, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadream.go BetaDreamService.New and BetaDreamService.Get and BetaDreamService.List and BetaDreamService.Archive and BetaDreamService.Cancel, and the CLI driving those same SDK methods, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betadream.go handleBetaDreamsCreate and handleBetaDreamsRetrieve and handleBetaDreamsList and handleBetaDreamsArchive and handleBetaDreamsCancel; platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide ("doesn't grant access to dreams"); internal/api/files.go fileJSON, internal/api/skills.go (skillJSON, one shape on every lane); CLAUDE.md 'Wire-compatibility rules'; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (api/server.go: '?beta=true and anthropic-* headers accepted and ignored')*
- **All endpoints — no `anthropic-workspace-id` response header, and the request header of the same name is accepted and ignored (plan 36 slice 0)** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — apierror.go Error.WorkspaceID and Error.Error, the SDK reads an `anthropic-workspace-id` response header on the error paths, unary and SSE alike, and appends it to the error string as `(Workspace-ID: …)`. This platform emits `request-id` on every response, success and error, and no workspace header — v1 is single-tenant and the workspace scoping key is reserved, not populated, so there is no id to name. The cost is one missing parenthetical in an SDK error message; the field decodes as empty and nothing branches on it. **The request header is the same posture seen from the other side.** The reference reads it to choose the workspace a request acts in — "Required with a multi-workspace API key. Optional for other API keys." — refusing a malformed value with 400 `invalid_request_error` ("anthropic-workspace-id header must be a valid workspace ID.") and an unknown or inaccessible workspace with 404 `not_found_error` ("Workspace `<id>` not found."), and the `ant` CLI sends it on the management lane beside `--api-key` and on the worker lane beside a Bearer environment key. **A 2026-09-05 multi-workspace recording puts all four arms on the wire** and adds three things the pages do not say: a workspace-scoped key naming a *foreign* workspace is refused 404 rather than served (the reading that made this arm inferable at all), the literal `default` is refused 400 like any other invalid value, and the 404's message echoes a **decoded UUID** rather than the id it was sent — it prints one even for a `wrkspc_` id belonging to no workspace, so it is decoding the input, not looking it up. When plan 42 slice 1 honors this header it will echo the id it was given instead, which keeps existence equally hidden without publishing a value the caller did not send; that half becomes its own entry when it lands. With one workspace there is nothing to select, so this platform reads the header nowhere — the string occurs in no Go file — and a malformed or foreign value is served rather than refused. Both halves end together: plan 42 slice 1 emits this header and `anthropic-organization-id` wherever a scope resolves, and honors the request header under one rule, that it may only narrow to a workspace the credential already covers. *Evidence: the field and the message suffix, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — apierror.go Error.WorkspaceID and Error.Error, and the two read points, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — requestconfig.go RequestConfig.Execute and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — ssestream.go richErrorDecoder.newAPIError; platform.claude.com api/overview (the request-header table) and the authentication doc (the two rejection bodies), fetched 2026-09-03; the header sent with `--api-key` and with a Bearer environment key, checked against anthropic-cli v1.26.1 — workspace_test.go TestWorkspaceIDHeader and TestWorkspaceIDHeaderOnWorker; internal/api/server.go:699 (withRequestID, the platform's only stamped response header), internal/api/auth_test.go (both the success and error assertions); a grep for the header name over this repository's Go files returns nothing; the 2026-09-05 multi-workspace recording (`2026-09-05/batch9.curl.txt` probes `item4.C.*`, and `batch8.json` for the response-header schedule), which also fixes `anthropic-organization-id`'s live shape as a bare UUID and shows the reference deciding both headers on two axes rather than one schedule — whether a route stamps at all comes first (`/v1/environments`, the work-poll lane and the token routes stamp neither, even at 200), and only within a stamping route does how far the request got decide which appear.* *Tracked: #56 (both halves — plan 42 slice 1 emits the response headers and honors the request header).*
- **Model backend (brain -> provider) — pluggable, config-driven providers** — The model backend is constructed purely from config (protocol anthropic|openai, model, base_url, api_key, optional headers) and routed via a model_providers registry. base_url is required for both adapters — there is no silent api.anthropic.com fallback — and an OpenAI-compatible gateway is a first-class backend. The reference has a fixed Anthropic backend; this whole seam is a deliberate self-hosting divergence. *Evidence: CLAUDE.md principle #4; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/provider` — config-driven model access (slice 4)" (provider/provider.go, provider/anthropic: 'base_url is required (no silent api.anthropic.com fallback)'); docs/history/2026-07.md § "OpenAI-compatible adapter (`internal/provider/openai`)"*
- **Model turn timeout (brain -> provider) — bounded by the endpoint's silence, per route** — A turn is abandoned once the endpoint has sent nothing at all for that route's `stall_timeout` (default 10 minutes; any byte received, keepalives included, buys the budget back), and surfaces through the session.error(model_request_failed_error) shape a failed model request already uses — no new wire surface — settling exactly as one does: idle with retries_exhausted, or a chained turn with retry_status retrying when input arrived mid-turn (see the session.error retry-semantics inference below). The reference's own timeout policy is unobservable: the SDK types carry none and no recording shows one. This is therefore a platform-side operational bound rather than a claim about the reference — the hosted reference bounds its own fixed backend however it likes, while a self-hosted deployment points at arbitrary endpoints and must not let one wedged proxy hold a brain replica out of service (#121). *Evidence: docs/plan/17_model-endpoint-stall-bound.md; internal/provider/stall.go (StallGuard); docs/HISTORY.md § "Model endpoint stall bound (plan 17)"; see also the session.error retry-semantics inference below (#78)*
- **Observability — OTel alongside span.* events; traceparent across processes** — OTel spans and Anthropic span.* wire events are emitted from the same instrumentation point; W3C traceparent/tracestate propagates through every cross-process call including through work items to BYOC workers. Additive platform surface (deliberate divergence goal: OTel built in). *Evidence: CLAUDE.md principle #3; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (events/span.go), docs/history/2026-07.md § "BYOC worker — `traceparent` propagation across the process boundary (slice 8, PR C2b-2)" (PR C2b-2, one OTel trace across the process boundary)*
- **Session resource field stats** — Rendered as its zeroed wire shape only — no backing computation exists. The wire shape is satisfied; the functionality is deferred. (outcome_evaluations left this entry with plan 21 slice 2, and deployment_id with plan 37 slice 3 — both are now real, stored state.) *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations" (wire-drift note, resolved by slice 2); reference shape checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.Stats and BetaManagedAgentsSessionStats*
- **Environment scope field** — scope is accepted and rendered only as the constant "organization"; "account" is rejected with a clear error (single-tenant v1). *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations", docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" ('scope rendered as the constant "organization"'), docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (deliberate v1 rejections)*
- **Environment `description` — a string that is never null (plan 36 slice 0)** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, the SDK spelled out semantics the earlier pins left unstated: the response field is "null when unset", and on update "Omit to preserve; null clears to null; an empty string is stored as an empty string". This platform's column is a non-null `text` and the field renders as a plain string, so an unset description is `""` rather than `null` and an update sending `null` clears to `""` rather than to `null`; omit-to-preserve and empty-string-stored-verbatim both match. The reference's own Go type is a bare `string` too, so the SDK decodes either shape and no client breaks — the divergence is what a byte-comparing recording would see. *Evidence: the response doc and the update doc, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, both sentences absent from the text checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, which read only "User-provided description for the environment" / "Updated description of the environment"; internal/api/environments.go createEnvironment and updateEnvironment (the two `if null { description = "" }` branches), renderEnvironment (the plain-string field)*
- **POST /v1/sessions (create) — multiagent config, resources** — Deliberate v1 rejections with clear errors. **The multiagent rejection is lifted by plan 35 slice 1:** an agent's `multiagent` roster is accepted and resolved at create/update (pinned `{id, type:"agent", version}` references, `self` to the coordinator's own id and the version the write produces, the documented constraints each a 400 — `internal/api/roster.go`), and a session snapshots it as full member definitions in `agent.multiagent`; slices 3 and 4 then made the roster load-bearing — its members spawn as real session threads the six delegation tools drive — and an advisor entry is rejected (#431, settled below). **The `resources[]` rejection is carved down three times — plan 08 slice 2 (files), plan 25 slice 1 (repos), plan 36 slice 3 (memory stores):** `type:"file"` and `type:"github_repository"` resources are accepted (materialized as `sesrsc_` mounts; a repo's write-only token seals into `session_resource_credentials`), and `type:"memory_store"` is accepted on a `cloud` session as the id-less element the reference documents, snapshotted from the store at attach time (the plan-36 entries below carry its readings) — and accepted on a `self_hosted` session the same way since plan 36 slice 6, where the BYOC worker mounts it from the per-item sessions token (its own entry below). **The `vault_ids` rejection is lifted by plan 12 slice 3:** a session now attaches vaults at create time (each id must name an existing, unarchived vault; the list round-trips and updates still reject it — see the session-attachment inference below). *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" ('Deliberate v1 rejections (documented divergences, clear errors)'); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseResourceObject / parseMemoryResource / snapshotMemoryStore; internal/api/sessions.go parseVaultIDs/validateAttachedVaults* *Tracked: #55 (delivered; files with plan 08, repos with plan 25), #50 (delivered; session vault attachment with plan 12 slice 3), #52 (delivered; memory stores with plan 36, whose slice 6 also made `self_hosted` sessions accept them).*
- **GET /v1/sessions list filter deployment_id — now a real filter (plan 37 slice 3)** — The filter no longer short-circuits to an empty page: sessions carry an optional, nullable `deployment_id` — written only by a deployment fire, emitted as an explicit null when unset, which the reference's optional key admits — and the filter is a keyset predicate over `sessions_deployment_idx`. A shape-valid unknown id is a 200 with an empty page, the rule the reference publishes for the runs list and we apply to both; a shape-invalid id is a 400, which the reference publishes for neither. The `memory_store_id` filter that shared this entry until plan 36 slice 3 has been real since then: a containment match on the session's `resources` array (the file-mount lookup's precedent) that answers every session whose element names the store — a deleted store's included, the element being a snapshot — and a 400 for a value not shaped like a `memstore_` id. *Evidence: the SDK's sessions-list `deployment_id` parameter ("Filter sessions created by this deployment ID"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaSessionListParams.DeploymentID, and its runs-list rule ("Filtering by a non-existent deployment_id returns 200 with empty data"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betadeploymentrun.go BetaDeploymentRunListParams.DeploymentID; `BetaManagedAgentsSession`'s 17 properties, 16 required, `deployment_id` the exception, string-or-null, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsSession.properties.deployment_id; `DeploymentID` (`api:"nullable"`) and the memory_store_id param, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.DeploymentID and BetaSessionListParams.MemoryStoreID; internal/api/sessions.go listSessions, internal/api/deploymentruns_test.go TestSessionsListFiltersByDeployment, internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionListFiltersByMemoryStore*
- **Anthropic prebuilt skills — operator-imported, date-versioned** — The reference hosts its prebuilt skill catalog itself (short-name ids `docx`/`pdf`/`pptx`/`xlsx`, date-based versions); a self-hosted platform has no Anthropic-run catalog to reach, so the controlplane's run-once `-import-anthropic-skills` mode provisions them instead: the operator points it at a local checkout of github.com/anthropics/skills, each directory is validated exactly like an upload, and rows land as `source='anthropic'` with the SKILL.md name as the id and the checkout's last commit date (`YYYYMMDD`, `-import-version` overrides) as the version — this platform mints those versions; the reference's own version values for the same skills will differ. `display_name` is likewise set to the short-name id (`xlsx`, not a marketing name) — the reference's actual `display_name` for these skills is unrecorded and may be human-readable (the 2026-09-04 recording listed the four prebuilt skills but exported only the key set, not their values). Anthropic skills are not API-manageable here (`POST …/versions` on one is a 400, and so is `DELETE` of the skill or a version — an accidental delete must not empty a catalog only the importer can refill), matching the reference's server-managed stance. *Evidence: docs/plan/06_skills.md design decision 4 + slice 3; cmd/controlplane (runImport), internal/api/skillsimport.go; managed-agents/skills docs (anthropic skill ids and date versions)* *Tracked: #78 (the reference's own display_name for these skills stays unrecorded); landed for #54.*

- **Skills materialization — sentinel idempotence instead of the reference's re-extract-every-item** — The reference's agent toolset re-downloads and re-extracts every skill each work item, clearing each destination and removing the per-skill directories it downloaded at the end, its workdir being host-shared — and the platform-managed sandbox extracts the same way but mounts the archives rather than fetching them, a split the recording paragraph below draws out; our per-session sandboxes instead keep a `.materialized` marker — the resolved `{skill_id: version}` set (never the landing directory) plus an integrity generation (`skills.SentinelVersion`, now 2 = every recorded archive was digest-verified where a digest existed) — and skip rewriting only when the marker is of the current generation, is an exact bijection with the freshly resolved set, and every skill's directory still holds its SKILL.md; a marker of a weaker generation never satisfies a stronger pass (one re-materialization per live sandbox at upgrade). The agent-writable workdir means the marker is never trusted for anything load-bearing, and three tampering residuals are accepted rather than closed by abandoning the skip as the reference's every-item re-extract does, which clobbers such edits every item: an in-place content edit; a partial deletion leaving the root SKILL.md the probe checks as a proxy for the tree; and — the landing directory being the skill *name*, shared across versions — a forged marker version over an older version's files, suppressing that one in-session upgrade. All three are one irreducible class rather than three unfixed bugs — an agent that controls the sandbox filesystem can always present chosen bytes under `skills/<name>/` — each persists only until the resolved set changes, and none reaches past the agent's own single-tenant session. We also rewrite in place without first clearing the directory (the sandbox seam has no delete primitive), so a file dropped between versions can linger as an orphan beside the new version's complete set — accepted, our workdir never being shared. The read verifies the archive against the sha256 recorded at upload — a further divergence from a reference that records and checks none — refusing a bit-rotted, truncated or substituted object; the mismatch is skipped per-skill under its own `corrupt` outcome (beyond plan 06's ok/not_found/failed) and a version predating the digest column is read unverified. Extraction takes zip only (the reference's tar fallbacks are dead code here) and, like the reference (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.0 — skillarchive.go zipEntryIsPlain and tarEntryIsPlain), materializes only regular files and directories — a Unix-host symlink, FIFO or device entry is skipped, never written out as a file holding its link target (TestExtractSkipsNonPlainEntries); three further deltas are defensive, unreachable for archives we validated: duplicate references dedupe first-wins (the reference, re-extracting each, is last-wins); an unusable version name falls back to the skill id (the reference tries `filepath.Base` first); traversal/backslash/relative segments are refused outright, where the reference absorbs them at the extraction root. Shared with the reference, not a divergence: both name the directory after the version's name, so two skills whose names collide overwrite each other under `{workdir}/skills/{name}/`. *Evidence: Skills.Versions.Download per item, RemoveAll+extract, Cleanup and filepath.Base, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.SetupSkillsFromSession and AgentToolContext.downloadSkill and AgentToolContext.Cleanup, and tar, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skillarchive.go extractTar; internal/executor/skills.go, internal/worker/skills.go; the derivations cut from here are comments in internal/skills/extract.go (SentinelVersion, ParseSentinel, SentinelMatches, Resolved, MaxArchiveBytes), pinned by TestSentinel, TestSentinelMatches, TestReadArchiveVerifiesDigest, TestExtractRefusals, TestTargetDir, TestMaterializePreVerificationSentinelIsNotTrusted* **A 2026-09-03 recording splits "the reference" here in two, and the first reading of it written in this entry was wrong.** Client-side, the SDK's agent toolset does re-fetch: it calls `Skills.Versions.Download` per skill per work item into `{workdir}/skills/{dirname}`, which is the sentence this entry opens with and which stands. The platform-managed sandbox does it differently — the zips arrive on a read-only rclone FUSE mount at `/mnt/skills`, the mount table being in the file-mount materialization entry below, which the same probe settled and which should stay the one place it is written down. What the recording does **not** show is the extraction going away: the Level-1 skill-injection entry, from this same wave, records `/workspace/skills/` holding the *extracted* `pdf/` and `xlsx/` trees, root-owned and not a symlink. So on neither path does the reference skip the per-item extraction, and the sentinel is not solving a problem the reference does not have — it answers whether a re-provisioned sandbox must redo that extraction, which is a live question on both. Nothing about the decision changes — a mount seam is a much larger commitment than an extraction seam — but the argument runs the opposite way from the one first written here: where the archives are a local mount, re-extracting is a copy rather than a download, so the managed side affords its clobber-every-item policy more cheaply than we would, and our skip rests on the sandbox being per-session rather than on saving a fetch. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probe `session.events.list.skill-paths`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §H.* *Tracked: #54 (delivered).*
- **Agent skills[] — accepted and normalized while distribution and execution were deferred** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (#54).** The normalization itself never was one: entries are validated and re-normalized to `{type, skill_id, version}` with an omitted version becoming the literal "latest" — which **matches** the reference: an omitted version "Defaults to `latest` when omitted", agent create accepts the literal `version:"latest"`, the alias persists unresolved into the agent and the session's agent snapshot, and it is resolved only at skill-materialization time, in the reference's own worker — the alias reaches that worker intact, and who turns it into an id once there is what changed under us. As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion, the worker did it itself, listing a skill's versions and picking the newest on the strength of a doc comment claiming the versions endpoints reject an alias, and downloading by the token that produced. That function was later removed: the SDK now retrieves the version the alias addresses and downloads by the concrete id that call returned (`skillVersion` "may be the alias `latest`, which only the retrieve endpoint resolves"), which leaves the resolving to the server. So where it happens is unchanged and who performs it is not. **This platform already answers the newer flow**: a retrieve addressed by `latest` echoes the version row's own id — `skver_` for a row minted since GA, the legacy `skillver_` for one an upgrade retained — and the download accepts either, on the bare and `?beta=true` paths alike. **Our BYOC worker keeps one delta from that flow**: the reference downloads by the retrieve's id for *every* pin, where ours does so only for the alias and carries a pre-GA numeric pin through verbatim (plan 39 decision 5). Both tokens address the same version and this platform's `/content` takes either, so it is another value a byte-comparing recording would see rather than a client that breaks. **This entry replaces an earlier CONFIRMED one claiming the reference resolves the version at create** — that claim was wrong; the entry is corrected rather than deleted so the reversal stays auditable. The divergence that correction left standing was the deferral behind the field: no /v1/skills API, no object storage, no sandbox materialization and no prompt injection existed here, where the reference had all four. The deferral is now closed: the /v1/skills registry and its object storage exist (plan slice 2), both halves materialize skills into sandboxes with use-time resolution (slice 4), and the brain injects Level-1 metadata into the system prompt at request-assembly time (slice 5 — see the injection-template entry in the INFERRED section below). **The literal the reference echoes after an omitted version is now recorded, and it is ours**: an agent created with `{type:"custom", skill_id}` and no `version` stores and returns `"version": "latest"`, as does one that sent the literal itself, and a `skver_` id is stored and echoed unchanged — the same normalization this platform has always performed, and it survives an archive. *Evidence: platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/skills ("Attach skills to an agent" field table: version "Defaults to `latest` when omitted"; create examples passing `version:"latest"`); the 2026-09-04 recording [106] (omitted → `"latest"`), [100] (the literal), [101, 107] (a `skver_` id, on create and on the later GET), [117-119] (the archive responses, unchanged); the retrieve, and the comment and the download by the id it returned, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.downloadSkill, against resolveSkillVersion, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion and absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion; internal/api/skills.go getSkillVersion and resolveSkillVersion, internal/api/skills_test.go TestSkillLatestAliasRoundTripsToDownload, internal/worker/skills.go resolveSkillVersion (the numeric-pin delta); docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (wire.go skill re-normalization)* *Landed for #54.*
- **POST /v1/skills — `display_name`'s derivation, its cap and its uniqueness** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (plan 39 decision 7).** The entry this replaces registered three inferences about a field it called `display_title`: that an omitted form value defaults to the SKILL.md frontmatter `name` verbatim, that uniqueness among a workspace's custom skills is enforced case-sensitively by a partial unique index exempting `source='anthropic'`, and that a duplicate is a 400. The 2026-09-04 recording answers all three, and renames the field on the way. It is `display_name`; omitted, it does derive from the frontmatter `name`; it is capped at 255 characters — 255 accepted, 256 refused with `display_name must be at most 255 characters long` — where this platform capped at 4096; and it is **not** unique, two skills having been accepted carrying the identical value. The uniqueness half was therefore not a conservative guess but a break, since refusing a create the reference accepts is harder for a client to work around than accepting one it refuses. Migration 0033 drops `skills_custom_display_title_uq`; the column keeps its 0007 spelling, only the wire field having been renamed. Nothing survives the convergence, and that closure is the record. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [2] (derived from frontmatter), [3, 4] (two skills, one `display_name`), [6, 7] (255 accepted, 256 refused with that message); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 7; internal/api/skills.go skillJSON, internal/api/skillsupload.go maxDisplayNameChars, internal/store/migrations/0033_skills_display_name.sql.* *Landed for #566.*
- **`…/versions/{version}` — the numeric version and the legacy `skillver_` id are accepted here and rejected there (plan 39 decision 4)** — GA addresses a version by its id, or by `latest` on the read routes: both answer 200, while a numeric timestamp is `Invalid version id: '1759178010641129'` and a `skillver_`-prefixed id is `Invalid version id: 'skillver_…'`. This platform's slot takes four forms — `skver_…`, `skillver_…`, `latest` on the read routes, and `^[0-9]{1,32}$` — the first and third matching GA, the other two additive and both deliberately so. Agents created before this convergence hold numeric pins, and a stored pin that silently stops resolving is a wrong answer rather than a refusal; `skillver_` is the id this platform minted for rows it does not rewrite (the prefix entry below). Where the reference refuses the alias, its own sentence is reproduced verbatim: `'latest' is not accepted when deleting a skill version. Address a specific version id: read the skill's latest_version_id, or GET /v1/skills/{skill_id}/versions/latest, and use that id.` on DELETE, and its download-worded twin on `/content`. One reference asymmetry is recorded rather than designed around: `/content` **does** accept the numeric on the GA lane where version metadata rejects it, which is the reference's behavior and not a rule we derived — this platform accepts the numeric on both routes, which is the wider of the two and what its own legacy-alias rule already requires. The operator importer keeps a version regex of its own, so widening the slot does not loosen it. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [30, 32] (id and `latest`, 200), [34] (the numeric refused by version metadata), [38, 149] (the `skillver_` id refused), [60] (the delete sentence), [45, 136] (the download twin), [150] (the numeric accepted by `/content` on the GA lane); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 4; internal/api/skills.go checkSkillVersion (skillVersionIDRe, skillVersionNumberRe), internal/api/skillsimport.go.* *Landed for #566.*
- **Skill version ids — `skver_` is minted, `skillver_` is accepted forever, and rows written before the convergence still render it (plan 39 decision 9)** — The reference's GA prefix is `skver_`, and its own pre-migration prefix was `skill_version_`. This platform minted `skillver_`, which matched **neither** lane, so the mismatch was always two-sided. It now mints `skver_` and keeps `skillver_` in `knownPrefixes` as an input alias, in the mould of the `session_` alias — but existing rows are deliberately **not** rewritten, because an agent's `skills[]` pins a version by id and a rewrite would dangle every stored pin. A skill version created before this change therefore still renders a `skillver_` id on the wire, where the reference would never emit one; a GA client round-trips it correctly, the id being opaque to it, and the reference refuses that spelling in its own `{version}` slot. What remains is a value a byte-comparing recording would see, not a client that breaks. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [2, 30] (`skver_` on the GA lane), [142] (`skill_version_` on the dated lane), [38] (the reference refusing `skillver_`); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 9; internal/domain/id.go (PrefixSkillVersion, altSkillVersionPrefix, knownPrefixes), CLAUDE.md's prefix list.* *Landed for #566.*
- **Agent `skills[]` — a dangling reference is accepted at create here and refused there (confirmed 2026-09-04)** — The reference validates a skill reference when the agent is written: a version that does not resolve is ``Agent has invalid configuration: `skill_id` `…` version `…` not found`` — its answer to a numeric pin, to a `skillver_` pin, to another skill's version id, and, once the skill itself is deleted, even to the alias `latest`. A reference that does resolve is stored and echoed verbatim. This platform accepts every one of those at create and at session override, and surfaces a miss late instead, at materialization, as a logged skip counted by `skills.resolve.misses`. The entry this replaces filed the question as an inference — "whether the reference also accepts a dangling reference at create is unrecorded" — argued from the SDK typing `skill_not_found_error` as a *deployment pause* reason, i.e. late-bound. The recording refutes it: the late binding is real on the reference too, but it is not the whole of its behavior. Adopting the create-time refusal is explicitly **out of scope for plan 39**, which converges the wire shape and adds no validation, so the permissiveness is registered rather than fixed. It is the safe direction of the two — every agent the reference accepts is accepted here, so no client is refused a write it could make there — and what a client loses is only the early error: a dangling pin here becomes a session that runs without the skill rather than a 400 at create. Deleting a skill an agent already references succeeds on both sides and leaves the agent holding a dangling reference, so the *state* is reachable there as well; the reference simply refuses to let a new agent be written into it. **How far its check reaches is narrower than it looks**: every recorded refusal is a `type:"custom"` reference, and the one `type:"anthropic"` reference probed — which named a *custom* skill's id, so it resolved to nothing an Anthropic catalog holds — was accepted and echoed with `"version": "latest"`. So the reference validates custom references at create; whether it validates anthropic ones at all is unobserved, and this platform validates neither. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [102, 103, 105] (numeric, `skillver_`, another skill's version id — each that message), [110] (`latest` after the skill is deleted), [100, 101, 106, 107] (the references that resolve), [104] (the anthropic-type reference accepted), [108, 109] (the delete that leaves an agent dangling); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md § "Out of scope"; internal/api/wire.go parseSkills (no existence check), internal/brain/skills.go, internal/executor/skills.go, internal/worker/skills.go (the late-bound miss).* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference's create-time check also runs on an agent update, on a session's agent overrides, and on an anthropic-type reference — none of which the recording probed — and with them whether this platform should adopt the refusal at all); landed for #566.*
- **`GET /v1/skills/{id}` on the environment-key lane — served here, refused there (confirmed 2026-09-04)** — The reference admits an environment key to the version list, to a version, and to `/content`, which is what the SDK's `SetupSkillsFromSession` doc comment prescribes, and refuses it on the **skill object** itself: `403 permission_error`, `OAuth token does not meet scope requirement any_of(org:skills, user:developer, user:managed_agents, user:skills, workspace:developer, workspace:skills)`. This platform serves the bare skill GET to the key as well, for a coherent read-lane rule (`isSkillReadPath`), and scopes nothing per environment because skills are workspace-global. The entry this replaces filed the reference's server-side scope as unrecorded; it is settled, and in the direction that makes ours the wider lane. It is left wide deliberately: the key already reads every version and every archive of the skill that object merely names, so narrowing it would withhold nothing it cannot reach by another route, and it would cost the dual-auth rule its one-sentence statement. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [143, 144, 145] (version, `latest`, version list on the key), [134, 135] (`/content` on the key, bare path and `?beta=true`), [146] (the skill object refused, with that scope message); the doc comment's WithHeaderDel("X-Api-Key") + WithAuthToken(environmentKey), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.SetupSkillsFromSession; internal/api/server.go isSkillReadPath and dualAuth.* *Tracked: #78 (whether a management x-api-key is admitted on those three routes there — the console-cookie refusal names "a workspace API key" among the credentials `/content` accepts, but no probe sent one); landed for #54.*
- **GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events/stream — history semantics** — The stream is a live tail from connect time: no history replay and no Last-Event-ID support. Reconnecting clients seed via the list endpoint. Safe because the reference client parses neither. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (slice-3 wire/scope decisions); anthropic-cli client behavior cited there*
- **POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — inbound `session_thread_id` (plan 35 slices 2–3)** — Accepted and validated since slice 3 (slice 2 rejected every non-null value while nothing could spawn a child): on `user.tool_confirmation`, `user.tool_result` and `user.custom_tool_result` the field is optional and, when given, must be the thread of the call it answers — the primary's own id for a primary call — else a 400; the answer is stored on that thread either way (routing is by the tool-use id, which the docs say to copy from the call). On `user.interrupt` it must name a live thread of this session — the primary's id means the primary, and interrupts that between them name every live thread (the primary's on a single-agent session) cancel every live item exactly as the unnamed form does; an archived thread or an unknown id is a 400 — and a value that is not an `sthr_` id is a 400 everywhere.
