What stays inferred is only the answer to a call other than `bash`, as above. A consequence worth naming, since it is a live behavior change in a package the gate does not otherwise touch: a session parked on an ask-gated MCP call is excluded from idle-TTL sandbox reaping like any other pending approval, even though the MCP call itself needs no sandbox — the session is mid-turn, and its built-in tools resume into the workspace the reap would have destroyed. Related: the `deny` EvaluatedPermission value is carried in the wire enum but never produced by any configurable policy. *Evidence: the confirmation's ToolUseID and SessionThreadID doc comments (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserToolConfirmationEvent.ToolUseID and BetaManagedAgentsUserToolConfirmationEvent.SessionThreadID); the MCP tool result — `mcp_tool_use_id` required, no `session_thread_id`, no `mcp_server_name` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentMCPToolResultEvent); internal/events/toolflow.go (confirmableToolUseTypes, toolUseAnswer, DenialResults, denialText); internal/events/denial_test.go (TestDenialAnswersInTheDeniedCallsOwnFamily, TestDenialWithoutAMessageStillCarriesText), internal/api/confirmation_test.go (TestConfirmationDenyAnswersWithErrorAndResumesBrain, TestConfirmationDenyOfAnMCPCallAnswersInItsOwnFamily, TestConfirmationDenialsKeepEachCallInItsOwnFamily, TestMCPCallBlocksTheRequiresActionGate).* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings — 2026-09-12/batch1.json `rec91.denial.events.with-message` (idx 82) and `rec91.denial.nomessage.events.result` (idx 90); 2026-09-12-followups/budget-runtime.json `rec.budget.confirmation.events.after-denial` (idx 16); 2026-09-19-custom-order-followup/setup.json `ask-first-deny.final-audit.events` (idx 63); 2026-09-19-custom-mixed-tools/setup.json `ask-custom-first-deny.final-audit.events` (idx 198) and `ask-deny-first-custom.final-audit.events` (idx 201).* *Tracked: #78 (a recorded denial of an MCP call, or of a built-in other than `bash`, would settle whether the sentence names it the same way), #793 (the result's place behind the resume's running pair); the MCP-family half landed for #45, the recorded `bash` text for #60.*
- **Tool-result content — the empty text block is refused, and a silent success posts `(no output)` (plan 35 slice 0)** — Every inbound text block — in `user.message` (its plain-string content form included: `content: ""` is refused too, since replay renders it as one text block), `user.tool_result`, `user.custom_tool_result`, a `search_result`'s nested content, and `system.message` (whose text folds into the system string, where empty is harmless — it shares the rule for uniformity) — must be non-empty (400 `text block text must not be empty` / `content must not be empty`), and both platform halves answer a tool that succeeded with no output with the text block `(no output)` (`toolset.NoOutput`; the executor's `agent.tool_result` and the BYOC worker's `user.tool_result` alike). The rule and the wording are the reference runner's own — "The Sessions API rejects empty text blocks; a tool that succeeds silently must still produce a postable result" — but the SDK witnesses it for tool results only and records no status; extending the refusal to `user.message` and `system.message` is the platform's inference, made because every user-side block is replayed to a Messages endpoint that rejects an empty text block, so accepting it would wedge the session on every later turn (the same hazard the MCP path already guards). **All three of those are recorded now** (2026-09-02): the reference refused an empty text block inside a `user.message` with 400 `invalid_request_error`, "events.0.user_message.content.0.text: value is required". So the status and type match ours, the refusal does reach `user.message`, and the platform's extension of the rule to that event is confirmed rather than inferred — the hazard argument above turns out to describe the reference's own behavior. Only the wording differs, and in a way worth keeping on both sides: the reference names the offending field path, ours names the rule. *Evidence: toToolResultContent, the "(no output)" substitution and its inline comment, and the function's doc comment that states the rule outright — "Empty text blocks are posted as `(no output)`" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessiontoolrunner.go toToolResultContent), and the two placeholder tests (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessiontoolrunner_test.go TestSessionToolRunner_EmptyTextResultPostsPlaceholder and TestToToolResultContent_EmptyTextBecomesPlaceholder); internal/events/inbound.go validateBlock; internal/executor/executor.go toolResultEvent; internal/worker/toolexec.go postToolResult; TestNormalizeInboundRejections ("text empty in …" rows), TestEmptyToolResultPostsPlaceholder (both packages)* *Tracked: #78 (the status, type and the refusal's reach into `user.message` are settled; what no probe covered is an empty `system.message`, whose text this platform refuses for uniformity alone — the recording refused a well-formed one on model grounds instead, "`system.message` events are not supported on model …", which this platform accepts unconditionally and #553 owns).*
- **Session resource — `budget` rendered, always null (plan 35 slice 0)** — the SDK added `budget` to `BetaManagedAgentsSession` as `api:"required"` without `nullable` (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.62.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.Budget); the platform has no budgets (#432) and renders the key as `null`, the way it renders `archived_at` (also required, also non-nullable in the tag) and the way the SDK's own deployment twin declares its `budget` (`api:"nullable"`). The reference renders `null` for a session created without one (recorded 2026-09-12), so the empty case agrees and the shape question this entry opened is answered; it stays here because the code is not — the key is null unconditionally, where a reference session carrying a budget renders it, and nothing here can yet produce that value. *Evidence: Session.Budget and the nullable SessionUpdatedEvent.Budget (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.Budget and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUpdatedEvent.Budget), and the nullable Deployment.Budget (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsDeployment.Budget); internal/api/sessions.go sessionJSON.Budget; sessions_test.go sessionRequiredFields* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-12/FINDINGS.md § "Budgets and usage" (a session created without `budget` returns `budget: null`).* *Tracked: #432 (budgets landing would give the key a value to render in place of the unconditional `null`).*
- **Environment kind mutability (cloud <-> self_hosted)** — Kind is immutable after creation: a config update flipping cloud<->self_hosted is rejected 400, because a kind change would re-home a session's compute and re-route its work queue between executor and BYOC worker mid-flight. The wire types do not reveal whether the reference forbids this — a conservative safety divergence, explicitly to confirm against a recording. Config updates within a kind are unaffected. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — environment-key auth + `/work/poll` (slice 8, first part)"; docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — environment-key auth + `/work/poll` (slice 8, first part)" (queue-split rationale)* *Tracked: #61.*
- **POST …/work/{work_id}/stop — graceful vs force semantics, and who completes the wind-down** — force:false/empty is read as 'ask the worker to stop' and force:true as 'stop now' (-> stopped). The wire exposes only {force?} and the state enum without spelling out the two-step wind-down, so every part of the graceful path below is ours to define. **The acknowledgement channel:** a heartbeat on a stopping item matches but does not extend the lease and reports the new state, which is how the worker learns. **Who leaves stopping:** the worker that was asked to stop — it winds its tools down and then force-stops the item itself (stopping -> stopped, stopped_at stamped, lease cleared). The reference worker reaches the same end state by force-stopping when its runner and heartbeat exit, unless its lease was lost (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — worker.go EnvironmentWorker.handleItem); ours must tell that stop apart from a lost lease too, because it also rotates work identities per re-hand-out (#62) and must never stop an item another worker may hold. **What may enter stopping:** only an active item, whose lease a heartbeat claimed. A graceful stop of a queued or starting item has no worker it can ask — a queued item has no holder at all, and a starting one does have a worker but no channel left to be told on, its only remaining beat being the claim, which the heartbeat refuses once the row is no longer starting — so it completes outright (-> stopped) rather than parking in a state nothing could leave. **Abandoned wind-downs:** a stopping item whose lease has lapsed — its worker died mid-wind-down — is finalized to stopped by the next Poll of its environment, never re-offered. Whether the reference's control plane admits queued/starting into stopping, and what finalizes an abandoned one there, is unrecorded; to confirm against a recording. *Evidence: internal/queue/lifecycle.go (Stop), internal/queue/queue.go (Poll's finalized CTE), internal/worker/lease.go (hbExit); internal/queue/lifecycle_test.go TestGracefulStopWithoutALeaseHolderStopsOutright + TestPollFinalizesAnAbandonedWindDown, internal/worker/lease_test.go TestWorkerControlPlaneStopWindsDown + TestWorkerLeaseLossDoesNotStopTheItem; docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — the work-item lifecycle: get / ack / heartbeat / stop (slice 8, second part)"* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the reference's own transitions); landed for #25.*
- **POST /v1/environments/{id}/work/poll (wrong method on the poll path)** — Folds into the {work_id} update route as work_id="poll" and — with a valid patch body — 404s on the nonexistent item (an invalid body is 400 first). Inferred from the SDK's own route table (POST v1/environments/{id}/work/{work_id} is the Update method, verified http.MethodPost, with a separate GET …/work/poll, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Update and BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Poll); whether the reference instead method-restricts the poll path to GET (405) is not decidable from the typed schema, and no real client issues the request. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — the work-item metadata update (slice 8, PR C-meta)" ('Assumption to confirm against a real recording'); the route table (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Update and BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Poll)* *Tracked: #63.*
- **Request bodies, path IDs, and query parameters — U+0000 (and any unstorable byte) rejected before it binds** — Postgres stores U+0000 in neither text nor jsonb, so the alternative to rejecting it is a 500 at bind time; we reject it in the shape an absent or unknown value already carries, on three surfaces. (1) **Bodies and inbound event payloads → 400:** every endpoint that decodes a JSON object body rejects U+0000 anywhere in it, key or value — not only the metadata-accepting ones; this covers a metadata *delete* key too, which the Go-side merges used to accept as a silent 200 no-op (the work endpoint 500'd on the identical patch) — the one genuinely wire-observable change in this family, every other surface merely converting a 500 into the status the wire already carried, accepted deliberately since that asymmetry was never a contract. (2) **Path IDs → 404, id-shaped query filters → 400:** a percent-decoded `%00`, like any non-alphabet byte, fails `domain.ID.Valid` before the bind and cannot name a stored row: 404 on every path id it validates, 400 on `agent_id` and on the `page` cursor's decoded id. (3) **Free-form `types[]` → 400 on the unstorable byte only:** an unknown-but-storable event type still filters to empty. The reference's behaviour is undecidable from the typed schema — opaque server-generated IDs, every other field (metadata included) a plain string — and no `ant` recording covers an unstorable byte, so whether it 400s, 404s, 500s or strips is unknown: stricter-than-schema by storage necessity, to confirm against a recording. **Outbound (#223):** a NUL a *tool* emits is stripped rather than rejected, so a stored `agent.tool_result` can differ from the tool's raw bytes by exactly its NULs; stripping is our choice, INFERRED to be at least as safe as the reject this entry records on the inbound side. **Model-side (#228):** the brain strips at its event-construction boundary — text deltas, the `tool_use` name and raw-JSON input, `failTurn`'s message — and that input's decode/re-encode also launders what only raw bytes can smuggle, a lone surrogate escape and invalid UTF-8, to U+FFFD. Both strips are ours and INFERRED: no recording shows what the reference does with NUL-bearing tool or model output — strip, replace, or fault, none of the three is covered. *Evidence: the derivations live in code comments — internal/api/wire.go rejectNULBody / checkID / storableText (SQLSTATEs, the single-chokepoint argument, the merge asymmetry), internal/domain/id.go Valid, internal/events/inbound.go rejectNUL, internal/toolset/toolset.go SanitizeText, internal/executor/executor.go toolResultEvent, internal/brain/stream.go normalizeToolInput, internal/brain/brain.go failTurn; pinned by internal/api/edge_test.go TestMetadataRejectsNUL / TestStringFieldsRejectNUL / TestPathAndQueryRejectNUL and internal/brain/nul_test.go; opaque-string IDs and map[string]string metadata (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgent.ID and BetaManagedAgentsAgent.Metadata, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.ID and BetaManagedAgentsSession.Metadata, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSelfHostedWork.ID and BetaSelfHostedWork.Metadata); docs/changelog/0.2.0.md § Fixed.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference rejects, strips or faults on a NUL); the outbound and model-side strips landed for #223 and #228.*
- **Query strings that do not parse — a 400 `invalid_request_error` wherever a lost pair would widen, swap or unguard the answer** — Go's query parser drops a pair it cannot decode — an invalid escape such as `%zz`, or a pair holding a bare `;` — and, past its default ceiling of 10,000 pairs (GODEBUG `urlmaxqueryparams`), the whole query, so a parameter sent that way read as never sent: a filtered list answered with everything it holds, `GET /v1/agents/{id}?version=1%zz` served the latest version, and `DELETE /v1/memory_stores/{id}/memories/{mid}` ran without its `expected_content_sha256` precondition. Those routes — the memory delete, the agent read, and the agent, session, session-event, deployment, deployment-run, dream, skill, file, memory-store, memory and memory-version lists — now refuse such a request with 400 `invalid_request_error` ("malformed query string: …"). So does the thread-event list, although its own parameters only page: it shares the session-event list's handler, which answers a filter key sent to a thread with a 400, and a filter key lost on the way would have answered unfiltered instead. The status, the message and where the line falls are ours: no recorded request's query string holds an undecodable escape or a `;` (every `path` value carrying a `?` in the recordings' JSON files, 5,475 of them), and the SDK cannot send one, since it builds its query strings with `url.Values.Encode`. It can pass the ceiling, though: 10,000 `types[]` values on the session-event list, beside the `beta=true` its path carries, are 10,001 pairs, and that request, which used to answer unfiltered, now draws the 400 too. The 400 is argued against the drop only, which answers a question the caller did not ask. A reference that read the bad pair literally, as WHATWG `URLSearchParams` and Python's `parse_qsl` do, would answer some of these requests as asked — `path_prefix=/notes;v2/` names a real prefix, since a memory path may hold a `;` — and against such a reference the 400 is stricter, not safer: it refuses a request its caller can repair only by percent-encoding the pair. The other routes still read their query leniently, since a pair lost there can change the page, the view, the preview frames or a timing, hide archived rows, or leave a refusal standing, but never answer with rows a filter excludes, serve another version, or let a guarded write through: paging alone, a view, `event_deltas`, the work API's timing knobs, `include_archived`, environment-delete `force`, and the heartbeat's `expected_last_heartbeat`, whose absence is already a 400. *Evidence: internal/api/page.go queryValues and the handlers that call it; internal/api/events.go listEvents (the thread-event list's filter-key refusal); internal/api/query_test.go TestMalformedQueryStringIsRefused / TestMalformedQueryStringKeepsTheDeletePrecondition; the SDK's query assembly (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — internal/requestconfig/requestconfig.go NewRequestConfig) and its event-list path and `types[]` spelling (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventService.List, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams.URLQuery); internal/memsync/memsync.go ValidatePath, which has no rule against `;`; managed-agents-wire-recordings, all eighteen sessions, searched for a request path whose query string holds a `%` without two hex digits or a `;`.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference answers a query string that does not parse with a 400, reads the bad pair leniently, or drops it).*
- **GET /v1/files — what the id cursors mean, and the unknown-cursor page** — Public docs settle more of this route than the SDK's types can, because the two dialects it serves are a documented compatibility affordance rather than a leftover: a request under `managed-agents-2026-04-01` gets a list that "includes `has_more`, `first_id`, and `last_id` alongside `next_page`", keeps `before_id`/`after_id` "still accepted (not combinable with `page` or `ids[]`)", and comes back "newest first" — the envelope, the exclusivity and the ordering all as implemented, and none of them this platform's invention. What the docs do not say is what the id cursors *mean*, and that half is still ours: `after_id` returns the rows immediately older than the cursor, `before_id` the rows immediately newer (fetched ascending, reversed to newest-first), an unknown one yields an empty page rather than a 400, and `next_page` is the position after the page in that same newest-first order — which on a `before_id` page is the cursor row's own, the one arm where `has_more`, answering the id lane's question instead, does not decide whether it is null. An **empty** page still sends the key and sends it `null`, as the one recorded empty response does; what it has no value for is the continuation, so a `before_id` that matched nothing reads as end-of-list where it is in fact the top of it; nothing can act on the difference, because a cursor pager stops on the empty `data` before reading the cursor. *Evidence: public docs https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/files § "Migrate from `files-api-2025-04-14`" (the managed-agents affordance) and § "List files"; docs/plan/08_files.md decision 8; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileListParams; internal/api/files.go listFiles* **`ids[]` is implemented and one reading of it is ours.** The docs settle the bound (100, counted after de-duplication), the exclusivity (`page`, `limit`, and the id cursors), the terminal page, and that ids which "do not resolve to a visible File — including deleted Files — are silently omitted". What they do not say is what a *malformed* id does. The scalar filters beside it — `deployment_id`, `memory_store_id`, `scope_id` — reject a bad shape with a 400 under #135's shape-first rule, but this one drops the entry instead, on the reading that an id of no valid shape resolves to no visible file by definition and that a batch parameter documented to tolerate misses should not fail a hundred-id request over one typo. The cost is that an all-malformed request answers 200 with an empty page rather than telling a client its ids are wrong. The grammar check is load-bearing for a second reason regardless: it is what keeps an unstorable byte out of the bind parameter, which #135 requires whatever the status code would be. The wire spelling is `ids[]` — `BetaFileListParams.URLQuery` pins `ArrayQueryFormatBrackets`, whose encoder appends `[]` to the key once per element (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileListParams.URLQuery, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — internal/apiquery/encoder.go encoder.newArrayTypeEncoder) — and the bare `ids` is accepted too, through the same `listParam` helper the other repeatable parameters use. An **empty** value is not an id here: `?ids=` reads as no filter rather than as a filter matching nothing, matching how every other empty parameter on this route reads, and the SDK's encoder emits no pair for an empty slice so it cannot produce one. The 100 bound counts entries the caller sent, de-duplicated, which means a malformed entry consumes cap and a hundred good ids plus one typo is a 400 rather than a quietly shortened page — the conservative side of a behavior no recording covers. *Evidence: the quoted contract is `BetaFileListParams.IDs`'s own doc comment, verbatim, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileListParams.IDs; the id-cursor sentence is the public-docs quote at the head of this entry; internal/api/files.go listFiles; internal/api/files_test.go TestFileListIDs.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of a paged file list would settle the id cursors' direction and the unknown-cursor page, and what the reference does with a malformed entry in `ids[]`); landed for #55, #544 and #652.*
- **POST /v1/files — multipart strictness and MIME derivation** — The upload requires exactly one part named `file`; extra, unknown, or duplicate parts are rejected 400. One parameter part is admitted beside it: `expires_in_seconds` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileUploadParams.ExpiresInSeconds), bounded 3600 to 7776000 as the public docs and the API reference both state, in either part order (#655, plan 49). **What is inferred is the refusal surface, not the parameter**: the docs publish the bounds and say nothing about what a value outside them, a non-integer, a padded one or a duplicate part reads as, so the 400 and its wording are this platform's, chosen to match the strictness the rest of this form already has. The filename follows the API reference's rule for the `file` part (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileUploadParams.File): only its final path component is kept, and an absent or empty one becomes `unnamed` plus the extension for the stored `mime_type`, when known. The SDK sends an empty one when `File` is given no name over a reader that has none, and `anonymous_file` for a bare reader. What the rule leaves open is ours: `/` and `\` both separate components — a `\` comes from the SDK itself, which on Windows names an open file by a `path.Base` that cuts only at `/` — so `dir/` and `dir\` leave an empty name, and the other documented forbidden characters apply to what remains; and the extension is the one the pinned table pairs with the type — `.jpg` for `image/jpeg`, `.txt` for `text/plain`, `.bin` for `application/octet-stream` (the one Python's `mimetypes` gives it too) — while a type the table does not list leaves the name a bare `unnamed` (plan 55). The stored `mime_type` is the part's declared Content-Type when specific, else the filename extension through the platform's pinned table (`internal/mimetab` — shared with the outputs harvest, so the fallback is host-independent and both writers of the `files` registry derive the same value for the same name, #264/#277), else `application/octet-stream`. A part with no Content-Type is taken whatever the beta header: the Files guide calls it "Required" under `files-api-2025-04-14`, a header this platform ignores like every beta header and which, the same guide says, the Go SDK stopped sending at its 1.68.0 release. The reference's exact rejection surface and error strings were never observed, and of its MIME derivation the Files guide says only that an omitted part Content-Type "is detected"; this platform sniffs nothing, so a nameless part without one — the SDK's `File` given neither a name nor a type — is stored as `unnamed.bin`, `application/octet-stream`. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md slice-1 inferences + "Recording checklist"; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileUploadParams; the SDK's filenames, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — field.go File, and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — internal/apiform/encoder.go encoder.newReaderTypeEncoder; platform.claude.com build-with-claude/files (fetched 2026-09-24 — the forbidden characters, and the migration table's part Content-Type row: "Required" with `files-api-2025-04-14`, "Optional; the type is detected when omitted" without); internal/api/filesupload.go (parseFileUpload, finalPathComponent); internal/mimetab/mimetab.go (ExtFor, preferredExt); internal/api/filesunnamed_test.go* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the multipart rejection surface, the extension a nameless upload takes, whether `\` separates path components, and the MIME fallback); landed for #55, for the parameter and the lifecycle behind it #655, and for the filename rule #738.*
- **GET /v1/files/{id}/content — download response headers and the not-downloadable status** — A downloadable file streams with Content-Type = its stored MIME type, Content-Length, and Content-Disposition naming the original filename (the CLI parses that filename; the exact header shape is inferred). An uploaded file (`downloadable:false`) is refused 400, matching the public docs ("Downloading a file you uploaded returns a 400 error"); the exact error string is this platform's own. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md decision 3 + slice-1 inferences; platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/files ("Downloading a file"); Content-Disposition parsing, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — cmdutil.go createDownloadFile; internal/api/files.go downloadFile* The refusal string is recorded now, from the console lane: ``File `file_…` is not downloadable. Only files generated by a tool (for example, the code execution tool) can be…`` — a 400, and it says something the plan did not assume, that **only tool-generated files are downloadable at all**, so an uploaded file is never a download whatever headers it would carry. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probe `cookie.files.content-original`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §A.* **A third status now shares this route and its precedence is inferred**: past `expires_at` the docs say plainly that "Downloading its content (`GET /v1/files/{file_id}/content`) returns a 404 error", but not where that check sits against the not-downloadable 400 and this platform's own environment-key mount gate. It is answered first here, so an expired file reads as absent on both lanes rather than as "not downloadable" on one and as bytes on the other; a recording of a content GET for an expired *upload* — where the reference must choose between 400 and 404 — would settle it (#655, plan 49). *Tracked: #78 (the Content-Disposition shape, which needs a download that actually succeeds — a tool-generated file, not the upload this probe used; and the expired-upload precedence above); landed for #55.*
- **DELETE /v1/files/{id} — dangling references tolerated** — Deleting a file still referenced by a session's `resources[]` is allowed (hard delete, no cascade or RESTRICT); later materialization records the missing file per-resource and the agent sees an absent mount, rather than the delete failing. The `files` row is authoritative for existence on both injection points: the brain's `resolveFilesBlock` joins the row and the executor's `materializeFile` checks it before streaming, so on any later injection or materialization pass a deleted file is dropped from the brain's next-turn block and its owed-to-the-drain object is not mounted onto a fresh or reprovisioned sandbox. It does not retract bytes an earlier pass already materialized into a live sandbox — those persist until the mounted set changes (the sentinel-idempotence residual below). **Expiry inherits that residual rather than tightening it** (#655, plan 49): past `expires_at` no pass mounts the file again on either half of the pull protocol, but a sandbox already holding the bytes keeps them, which is what the docs describe for deletion too — files "may persist in active Messages API calls and associated tool uses". Whether the reference restricts or tolerates this is unrecorded. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md decision 2 + "Recording checklist"; internal/api/files.go deleteFile, internal/executor/files.go materializeFile, internal/brain/files.go resolveFilesBlock* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference restricts or tolerates deleting a still-mounted file); landed for #55.*
- **POST /v1/sessions (create) and POST …/resources — file resource validation** — Rooting is CONFIRMED (#323), the rest INFERRED. Every supplied `mount_path` roots under the session's uploads directory — `/data.csv` and `data.csv` alike land at `/mnt/session/uploads/data.csv` — an omitted one is `/mnt/session/uploads/<file_id>`, parent directories are created automatically, and create and add resolve alike. Inferred: (1) **the already-rooted pass-through** is ours, a carve-out from the documented rule rather than a gap in it — read literally the rule roots an already-rooted path twice, so an already-rooted **absolute** path instead passes through cleaned but not re-rooted; the basis is the reference's own data-analyst cookbook, sample code rather than one of CLAUDE.md's three rungs, so this stays inferred. A *relative* spelling of the root is uncovered by that evidence and roots like any other; a recording of a session created with an already-rooted `mount_path`, read back from session GET, would close it. (2) A referenced file is proven to exist inside the create/add transaction — a missing file is a 404, not a deferred materialization miss, and an **expired** file is missing for this purpose (#655, plan 49): its content is contractually gone, so mounting it would place a file in the sandbox the content route refuses to serve. The docs' nearest sentence is about a different API — "A Messages request that references the file fails before inference" — which this platform does not serve, so that the reference refuses the mount too is an inference. (3) The **resolved** path must be ≤1024 bytes, storable text, and unique per session — a duplicate is 400, judged on the *cleaned resolved* path, so aliases of one mount collide (a pre-#323 non-canonical literal included). (4) A path that still climbs above the root after cleaning, or names the root itself, is a 400 — reaching only **relative** spellings, since an absolute path's `..` cleans away entirely. **One soft spot in the basis:** `/uploads/<name>` recurs across the reference's own artifacts (three SDK request fixtures, the SDK example's prompt) and doubles under this rule to `/mnt/session/uploads/uploads/…`; those fixtures assert only body mapping, so nothing contradicts the docs — but it is the second thing a recording should check. Rooting also means a `mount_path` created from now on cannot *name* a location outside the sandbox's writable set — lexically and for new resources only (no backfill; the agent-writable uploads directory still admits an intermediate symlink redirecting a mount's bytes, the mount sentinel's accepted single-tenant tampering residual, below). *Evidence: public docs https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/files § "File paths" and https://platform.claude.com/cookbook/managed-agents-data-analyst-agent; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsFileResourceParams, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionresource.go BetaManagedAgentsFileResource, and the `/uploads/…` spellings in the SDK's tests (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession_test.go TestBetaSessionNewWithOptionalParams; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionresource_test.go TestBetaSessionResourceAddWithOptionalParams; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment_test.go TestBetaDeploymentNewWithOptionalParams and TestBetaDeploymentUpdateWithOptionalParams) and the prompt in the SDK repository's examples/agents-with-files/main.go, a separate module the pin does not fetch (read in the checkout 2026-09-17); internal/api/sessionresources.go, whose resolveMountPath comment now holds the rooting, style-not-root, pass-through, clean-before-rooting and lexical-containment derivations dropped from here, pinned by TestResolveMountPath and TestMountPathTakenCleansStoredPaths (internal/api/unit_test.go) and TestSessionResourceValidation (internal/api/sessionresources_test.go); containment's two limits in docs/self-hosted-security.md; the pre-#323 behavior corrected here in docs/changelog/0.2.0.md. **docs/plan/08_files.md decision 6 is superseded on this point, not authority** — it still reads "must be absolute, NUL-free, ≤ 1024 bytes"; left unedited, an archived plan being a record, not a live spec.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of an already-rooted mount_path read back from session GET, and of the reference's own `/uploads/<name>` spelling, would settle the two checks this entry names); landed for #55.*
- **POST /v1/sessions (create) — github_repository resource validation and sealed token storage** — A `github_repository` resource is validated create-time-local, no network call proving the repo or token: `url` must be the exact canonical `https://github.com/{owner}/{repo}` (optional `.git`; no userinfo, port, query, fragment — a bare trailing `?` or `#` included — extra segments, or trailing slash — the url is a rendered, logged field, so every credential carrier is refused; both segments are bounded to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` and a repo name `path.Clean` would rewrite (`.`, `..`) is refused, so the derived default mount path is clean and storable by construction — and the derived default is validated like a supplied one); `authorization_token` must be non-empty and at most 8 KiB (the cap keeps every `secrets.Cipher` backend inside its plaintext ceiling); `checkout` is a strict `{type:"branch",name}` | `{type:"commit",sha}` union with `sha` exactly 40 hex, stored and rendered **as given — `null` when omitted**, the default branch resolving at clone time; `mount_path` defaults to `/workspace/<repo-name>` (last URL segment, `.git` stripped) and, when supplied, must equal its `path.Clean` form, not take a reserved target (`/`, `/tmp`, and from plan 36 slice 3 `/mnt/memory` or any path below it — the memory stores' parent, decision 8), not nest with another repo mount, and no resource may sit at a proper ancestor of a repo mount (a resource inside a repo mount stays legal; the cross-resource rules compare the file arm by its uploads-resolved path, #323); a session mounts at most eight repositories. The token itself never enters the verbatim-echoed `sessions.resources` jsonb — it seals through `secrets.Cipher` into `session_resource_credentials` (ciphertext + key id, session-cascade lifetime), and a cipher-less deployment refuses the create rather than storing anything. The SDK pins only the required fields, the mount-path default string, and the checkout variants; every strictness above, the as-given checkout rendering, the cap values, and the refusal shapes are unrecorded. *Evidence: docs/plan/25_git-repo-mounting.md decisions 1-3 + slice-1 inferences; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResourceParams and BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResourceParamsCheckoutUnion, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionresource.go BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResource, "write-only and never returned" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResourceConfig); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseRepoResource / parseGitHubRepoURL / insertSessionResourceCredentials; internal/store/migrations/0020_session_resource_credentials.sql* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the validation strictness, the as-given checkout rendering, the cap values and the refusal shapes); landed for #55.*
- **Session resource sub-endpoints — mutation gate, deletion semantics, and the token-rotation reject** — `sesrsc_` resources live in the `sessions.resources` jsonb array; the list endpoint returns all when `limit` is omitted (SDK: "if omitted, returns all") and paginates by the last-id cursor otherwise. Add and delete are mutations, rejected on an archived session (the session-update gate) but allowed regardless of running/terminated status; an added file also obeys create's no-resource-above-a-repo-mount rule; deletion removes the reference only and never unmounts a live sandbox. `POST …/resources/{rid}` (the token-rotation update) is **live for `github_repository` resources since plan 25 slice 1**: the new token re-seals over the stored ciphertext, the resource's `updated_at` bumps inside the echoed jsonb, and the full rendered resource returns; a file resource keeps the "only github_repository resources support token rotation" reject, an archived session the archived-mutation reject, and a cipher-less deployment refuses the rotation outright. `DELETE …/{rid}` on a repo resource is likewise rejected — "Repositories are attached for the lifetime of the session" (the public guide); the SDK's generic Delete proves only that the route exists. None of the rotation response shape, the repo-delete rejection, or their error strings are recorded. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md slice-2 inferences + "Recording checklist"; docs/plan/25_git-repo-mounting.md decisions 2-3; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionresource.go BetaSessionResourceUpdateParams; internal/api/sessionresources.go rotateResourceTokenTx / deleteSessionResourceTx* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the rotation response shape, the repo-delete rejection and their error strings); landed for #55.*
- **`github_repository` materialization — clone timing, contents, failure surface, and the Mounted-repositories block (plan 25 slice 2)** — The reference documents only that repositories are cloned into the sandbox; every operational property below is ours (INFERRED). **Timing:** the clone runs in the executor on the session's first work item, on our lazy-provision lifecycle (nothing exists to clone into before the first tool call); the reference may well provision eagerly at create. **What is fetched:** a full, non-shallow clone, no submodule recursion, no LFS smudging, with `checkout` resolved *here* rather than at create. Two descriptors create accepts are refused only at clone time, both ours: the **null commit id** (forty zeros) and a **branch name go-git cannot build a refspec from** (containing `:`); both report reason `checkout`. **Credentials:** the token rides each request's `Authorization` header (`x-access-token` basic auth), never the remote URL, so the stored `.git/config` is token-free and the sandbox never holds the credential in any form (a security property, but agent-observable). Consequently a **rotation is not retroactive**: future clones only, never a checkout already on disk. **Idempotence** is probe-only — `<mount>/.git` present means materialized, no sentinel file. **Failure:** a repository that will not clone records a `session.error` of payload `type` `github_repository_clone_error`, carrying `resource_id`, `mount_path`, `url`, a fixed human-readable `message`, a `reason` from `auth`/`not_found`/`network`/`checkout`/`too_large`/`timeout`/`internal`, and `retry_status` always `{"type":"retrying"}`, `auth` included; it is deduped per (resource, reason), and the session runs on with its other mounts. The variant name, its fields — bar `retry_status`, required on every variant of the reference's error union and carried by every other `session.error` this platform writes, so only its always-`retrying` value is ours — the dedupe and the tolerate-don't-fail stance are all ours (the reference surfaces clone failure in a way nothing records, possibly not as an event at all); `too_large`/`timeout` are ours twice over, since the budgets they report (`EXECUTOR_REPO_CLONE_MAX_BYTES`, `EXECUTOR_REPO_CLONE_TIMEOUT`) have no wire surface. **The "Mounted repositories" block** the brain appends to the system prompt — heading, `- <path> (<url>, branch X | commit SHA | default branch)` lines, placement after the skills and files blocks, `cloud`-only gating (the self_hosted entry above) — is inferred exactly as its Mounted-files twin below is, and for the same reason: the reference must tell the model where the checkout is, but nothing records how. *Evidence: docs/plan/25_git-repo-mounting.md decisions 1, 4-8 + slice-2 inferences; platform.claude.com managed-agents/github (fetched 2026-08-06 — mounting documented, mechanics not); the derivations cut from this entry live in comments in internal/executor/repoclone.go (per-checkout clone shape, null-sha substitution, refspec blame in cloneReason) and internal/executor/repos.go (materializeRepos, extractRepo, emitRepoCloneError), with internal/brain/repos.go for the block; pinned by internal/executor/repos_test.go's TestRepoZeroShaIsRefused, TestCloneReasonBlamesTheRightSide, TestRepoTokenNeverEntersTheSandbox, TestRepoRestoredCheckpointSkipsClone, TestRepoInterruptedExtractMidTarLeavesNoTree, TestRepoCloneFailuresSurface and TestRepoCloneErrorDedupe* **What the reference fetches, and its failure surface, are now recorded.** `authorization_token` is **required** — omitting it on a public repository is a 400, `resources.0.github_repository.authorization_token: value is required` — and **never verified**: with a deliberately wrong token, `https://github.com/anthropics/skills` cloned in full and **no `session.error` was emitted at all**, so a required field that is never checked invites callers to pass anything and a wrong token fails silently rather than loudly. From inside the checkout: `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` is **`true`**, so the clone is shallow; there are **two remotes**, `github` and `origin`, both the plain `https://github.com/anthropics/skills.git`; there is **no credential in the remote URL**; and `mount_path` is `/workspace/skills`, the repository name rebased under `/workspace`, which matches what this platform derives. **The first two do not match us** and were recorded here before they were checked: `repoclone.go` builds `git.CloneOptions` with no `Depth`, so our clone is **full**, and go-git's `Clone` creates the single remote `origin` where the reference carries two. #582 prices both — the shallow default against our commit-checkout case, which needs arbitrary history, and the second remote, which costs one call and makes a script written against the reference work here unchanged. One observation is recorded as an anomaly rather than a conclusion, because it is the opposite of what a fresh clone into a fresh sandbox would leave: the working tree's mtimes predate the session by eighty minutes while `.git` is stamped at clone time, which points at a shared or cached checkout — plausibly the same per-session filestore mount the skills entry records, unproven here. Clone **timing** is therefore the half still open, and the anomaly is what an eager-versus-lazy probe would have to explain. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `sess.create.repo-mount-anon` and `session.events.list.repo-inspect`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §I.* *Tracked: #78 (clone timing, the cached-checkout anomaly, and the Mounted-repositories block are unsettled; a valid read-only PAT would sharpen all three), #582 (price the shallow default against our commit-checkout case, and the second remote); landed for #55.*

- **Mounted-files block — the injection format and placement are inferred (the Level-1 skills twin)** — Both execution halves materialize a session's file mounts into the sandbox (the executor for platform-managed sessions, the BYOC worker for self-hosted ones); the brain additionally renders a "Mounted files" block into the system prompt so the agent can find mounts that live outside its workdir. The block is a lead line plus one `<mount_path> (<filename>, <mime_type>, <size> bytes)` bullet per mount, sourced from the session's `resources[]` joined to the `files` table, appended **after** the Level-1 skills block and before any runtime `system.message` text. A dangling mount (its file row gone) is a logged skip, never fatal. The reference captures skill metadata "in the system prompt" but no source captures any file-mount prompt text, so the wording, the placement, and whether the reference injects file metadata at all are inferences — the same open questions as the Level-1 skill injection entry above, and a recording of a session asked to echo its instructions would close them. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md decision 7 + slice-3 inferences; internal/brain/files.go (resolveFilesBlock, renderFilesBlock), internal/brain/replay.go (buildRequest placement)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of a session asked to echo its instructions would settle the wording, the placement, and whether the reference injects file metadata at all); landed for #55.*
- **File-mount materialization — sentinel idempotence, extended to mounts** — Both halves stream each mount's bytes to its `mount_path` before tools run — executor from object storage, BYOC worker over the content lane — recording a `{workdir}/.files_materialized` sentinel of the sorted `{file_id, mount_path}` set; re-provisioning the same live sandbox skips restreaming only when the marker equals the current set **and** every mount is still present. Two deltas from the skills sentinel, forced by arbitrary-sized mounts: presence is one `test -e` exec, not a read-back, so it checks existence, not content — a mount an agent truncates or overwrites in place is not healed until the mounted set changes (skills' accepted single-tenant tampering residual); and bytes go through `Sandbox.WriteFileStream`, not the buffered `WriteFiles` skills use. A dangling mount is skipped, never fatal, counted in `files.materialized` (`not_found`) and outside the `files_materialize` span's landed count — the skills precedent. Two accepted residuals, both single-tenant and self-healing: (1) **closed by #71** — both backends now stream to a temporary sibling and rename only once the byte count validates, so a stream that failed partway (a short body, a wrong `Content-Length`, a cancelled context, a full disk) leaves the target as it was rather than exposing truncated or previously-mounted bytes to the current work item, and the sentinel still excludes that mount for a later pass; (2) a mount at the sentinel's own path is honored (the file wins) but suppresses the sentinel: a `mountAtPath` guard disables the marker on **both** the skip-read and the write, so marker-equal bytes there cannot short-circuit re-materialization and the marker cannot clobber the file — that session re-materializes every pass. **Since #323 the guard is narrower than it reads:** mounts resolve under `/mnt/session/uploads` and the default workdir is `/workspace`, so only a path stored before #323 or a workdir configured under the uploads root can still collide. The reference worker has no file materialization at all — the mechanism is wholly ours. *Evidence: docs/plan/08_files.md decision 7, slices 3–4; derivations cut from here are comments in internal/{executor,worker}/files.go (materializeFiles/SetupFiles, mountsPresent, mountAtPath), pinned by TestFilesMaterializeIdempotent, TestFilesRematerializeAfterMountDeleted and Test{Files,SetupFiles}SentinelPathCollision; atomic write: internal/sandbox/sandbox.go's WriteFileStream doc, #71 argument in sandboxtest/contract.go WriteThatFailsLeavesTheTargetAlone; #323 rooting in internal/api/sessionresources.go resolveMountPath* **The recording dissolves this question rather than answering it, and the entry is kept only to say so.** The reference does not stream a file mount's bytes into a container at all: it mounts session-scoped object storage over rclone FUSE, keyed by the session id — `rclone-filestore:sesn_…:/mnt/skills` → `/mnt/skills` (ro), `…:/mnt/session/uploads` → `/mnt/session/uploads` (ro), and `…:/mnt/session/outputs` → `/mnt/session/outputs` (rw). So "does a re-provisioned sandbox re-stream a mount it already placed" has no referent there: a fresh sandbox re-mounts the same filestore path and the content is simply back. Skills are the one place bytes still land in the workdir — extracted out of `/mnt/skills` rather than fetched, as the skills-materialization entry above now records — so the mount removes the *fetch* everywhere and the in-container copy only here. The sentinel answers a question the reference does not have, which is a better argument for it than the one this entry was opened with, and the same mount model explains two things recorded elsewhere in the wave — that the outputs harvest reads `/mnt/session/outputs`, the one read-write mount, and that a `github_repository` checkout carries mtimes older than its session. One further fact fell out of the same probe and is not in the analysis file, read straight off the create response: **a `file` session resource echoes a different `file_id` than the one requested** — `file_01W2uSvi…` went in, the resource came back carrying `file_01UfkHfJ…`, while `mount_path` kept the *original* id as its filename (`/mnt/session/uploads/file_01W2uSvi…`). The reference copies the upload into a session-scoped object before mounting it, so the id a caller passes and the id the session holds are not the same id, and neither of them is downloadable on the environment-key lane. **`materializeResourceInputs` (`internal/api/sessionresources.go`) echoes `in.fileID` unchanged**, so `resources[].file_id` carries a different value here than there — a concrete wire difference, now #578. What stays ours and unrecorded is the dangling-mount arm. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `session.events.list.skill-paths` and `session.create.cloud-with-file` (entry 17's response body), analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §H.* *Tracked: #578 (the session-scoped file id `resources[]` echoes), #78 (what the reference does with a dangling mount; whether we adopt the mount model or keep staging is a design question rather than a recording one); landed for #55.*
- **`credential_host_unreachable_error` — the reference recorded, our emission point and policy lifetime not yet aligned (plan 12 slice 4c-2c)** — The SDK pins the shape (a variant of the `session.error` event's error union: `credential_id`, `vault_id`, `message`, `retry_status{type: retrying|exhausted|terminal}`, `type`) and the trigger ("An `environment_variable` credential's `auth.networking.allowed_hosts` includes a host the environment's network policy does not permit" — a *configuration conflict*, not a runtime substitution miss; runtime non-substitution on a non-allowed host is separately documented as normal, non-erroring behavior). Everything else is this platform's inference: **when** — detected at gate-config render (`GET /internal/v1/gate/config`), the first point that observes both configs for a session whose gate is live, re-evaluated every fetch so vault/policy edits track without a restart (the reference's detection point is unrecorded — create-time validation would also be defensible), and the append itself is detached from the fetch response — post-commit, its own 10-second budget, at most one in flight per session, never failing or delaying the fetch — so the event lands shortly after the config render that triggered it (externally observable in event ordering; the reference's delivery timing is likewise unrecorded); **cadence** — appended best-effort once per (session, credential) (an events-table check-then-append dedupe — a concurrent duplicate fetch can rarely double-emit), so a conflict is a signal, not a per-fetch drumbeat; **`retry_status`** — `{"type": "retrying"}`, chosen because detection re-runs and an edit heals the conflict (nothing is observed about when the reference reports `exhausted` or `terminal`); **`message`** — our own wording, naming the uncovered `allowed_hosts` entries (hostnames are non-secret); **entry coverage** — an exact entry is covered iff the policy matches it, while a `*.D` wildcard entry (a whole subdomain family) is covered only by a policy wildcard at or above `D` — a policy's exact host can never cover a family, and a broader wildcard is not covered by a narrower one (`egress.HostSet.CoversEntry`; the SDK sentence never says how a wildcard *entry* compares against a wildcard *policy*). A recording of the reference with a deliberately conflicting credential would confirm the emission point, cadence, `retry_status`, and message. *Evidence: BetaManagedAgentsCredentialHostUnreachableError and its doc comment (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsCredentialHostUnreachableError) and the networking docs (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betavaultcredential.go BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworkingParams and BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworkingResponse), the cited types unchanged since anthropic-sdk-go v1.59.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsCredentialHostUnreachableError and since anthropic-sdk-go v1.59.0 — betavaultcredential.go BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworkingParams and BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworkingResponse; internal/api/gateconfig.go emitUnreachableCredentials, internal/egress/hostset.go CoversEntry* **All four are now recorded, and two of them this platform does not match.** On the reference, emission is at session start, **before `thread_status_running`**, and it is **once per session, not once per turn** — three messages produced exactly one error and the second work cycle produced none, which is the sharp contrast with `mcp_connection_failed_error`'s every-cycle cadence and is coherent with it: a policy conflict is a static session-scoped fact, a dial is retried. It is **one error per offending credential**, two arriving back to back where a vault held two. `retry_status` is `retrying`. The key set is `credential_id, message, retry_status, type, vault_id` — no `mcp_server_name`, so the two error families do not share a shape. The message states a contract worth adopting verbatim: *a session's network policy is captured at creation and is not affected by later environment changes* (which is the question #297 is deciding for cloud environments). Coverage was probed as a truth table across four session creates, and it confirms `CoversEntry` rather than plain matching: an environment on `api.rec78.invalid` fails to cover a credential on `other.rec78.invalid` **and** one on `*.rec78.invalid`, while an environment on `*.rec78.invalid` covers both — so **a credential's wildcard is not satisfied by an exact host in the environment policy**, and the environment's patterns must cover the credential's entries using the same wildcard semantics the allowed_hosts entry records. **Where this platform differs**, and it is the emission rather than the classification: `emitUnreachableCredentials` runs from a **gate-config fetch**, in a goroutine `getGateConfig` detaches, so a session that never provokes one emits nothing at all — where the reference emits at session start regardless. And the conflict is computed against the **live** environment row, deliberately (*"resolution is read-time, so an edit heals or introduces a conflict without a restart"*), where the reference's own message says the policy is captured at creation and later environment edits do not reach a running session. Ours is also "best-effort once" per (session, credential) via a check-then-append that admits a double-emit under concurrent fetches, against a once-per-session the reference states outright. The classification, key set, `retry_status` and per-credential multiplicity do match. #579 owns the emission point and the cadence; the policy-lifetime half is the question #297 is already deciding for cloud environments, and the recorded contract is now attached there as evidence. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probe `session.events.list.cred-host-conflict` and the four creates beside it, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §F.* *Tracked: #579 (emit at session start against a creation-time snapshot, or argue the read-time model as a divergence); landed for #50.*
- **web_fetch / web_search — model-facing input schemas** — The definitions offered to the model are minimal and ours: `web_search {query: string, required}`, `web_fetch {url: string, required}`, descriptions authored fresh. The reference's model-facing schemas for these two tools are unobservable in every source — the SDK's client toolset implements only the six sandbox tools, and no recorded wire carries the managed backend's definitions — so the field names, the requiredness, and the absence of tuning knobs (a max-results count, a timeout) are inferences. A recording of a managed-agents session with the tools enabled, asked to echo its tool definitions, would close this. *Evidence: internal/toolset/definitions.go (the web_fetch/web_search entries); docs/plan/15_web-tools.md "Wire shapes"* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of the tools' definitions would settle the schemas); landed for #47.*
- **Mixed web+sandbox turns — web-first hold-back ordering** — A turn calling both a web tool and a sandbox tool serializes web-first: the brain's settlement (and the confirmation resume) enqueues only the `web_exec` item, and the executor's web driver chains the sandbox `tool_exec` once every web call is answered — so on self_hosted the worker's item does not exist until then. The ordering is ours, not a recorded reference behavior; whether the reference orders a mixed turn this way or runs the two sides in parallel is unobserved. **The third possibility is not**: a 2026-09-02 recording caught the reference mixing them, one assistant turn emitting `web_search` and `bash` together (1 µs apart in the log) and both results landing together too — so "never mixes them at all" is ruled out, while the two live readings stay open, since that bash was an instant `echo` on a `cloud` session and so distinguishes neither ordering nor parallelism. One half of the original rationale has since been *measured* and softened: the slice-3 acceptance's mixed turn showed the real `ant` worker (checked against anthropic-cli v1.21.0 — worker.go handleWorkerPoll), on scanning a web `agent.tool_use`, logging "tool not owned by this runner; leaving the tool_use_id pending for its owner" and running only its own bash call — this client tolerates a non-owned call rather than answering it unknown-tool (its answered-diff also did not count the already-committed platform `agent.tool_result`, so the ownership check, not the hold-back, is what spared it). The hold-back is retained regardless: it makes the ordering deterministic rather than dependent on every client version's tolerance, and a client that *does* answer the whole set would otherwise wedge the call. An outstanding MCP call now sits ahead of both in the same ordering, for a reason the entry below on answering an MCP call records: only this platform's driver can answer one. *Evidence: internal/brain/brain.go (commitTurn's kind choice), internal/api/events.go (the confirmation-resume kind choice), internal/executor/webwork.go (the chained tool_exec), internal/worker/toolexec.go (the IsWebTool filter); docs/HISTORY.md § "Web-tools slices 2+3 acceptance" (the observed worker log line)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference orders a mixed turn this way, runs the two sides in parallel, or never mixes them at all); landed for #47.*
- **user.define_outcome acceptance — everything past the typed schema and the documented rules (plan 21 slice 2)** — The SDK pins the shapes and the public docs pin the rules; the rest is ours, each item needing a recording. **Error shapes:** the one-at-a-time rejection, an out-of-range `max_iterations` (1–20; the docs say only "default 3, max 20"), a present-but-empty `description`, and an empty rubric `content`/`file_id` (the SDK pins only requiredness) are each a 400 `invalid_request_error` — its type the reference's, its message ours. **File rubrics (cap, scope policy and snapshot all ours):** the file must exist in the org scope (v1's single-tenant boundary) and fit a 256 KiB byte cap mirroring the SDK-documented 262144-character text bound, which we count in runes; accepted bytes are snapshotted at acceptance to an outcome-owned key (`outcomes/{outcome_id}/rubric`). **Interrupt settlement:** a non-terminal outcome flips to `interrupted` with a terminal `span.outcome_evaluation_end`: `outcome_evaluation_start_id` empty when no grading cycle had started (documented), the committed `span.outcome_evaluation_start` when the entry is `evaluating` (the lone carve-out of three non-terminal results); the entry's current `iteration` (0 with no cycle — emitted even on an end that had no grading cycle, the SDK tying the field to a start event this event does not have); our explanation text and a zero `usage`. All four are SDK-required fields, so only their values are ours. **Archive settlement (ours; the docs say nothing of a live outcome on archive):** archiving a session ends a live outcome the same way, with the same end event, explanation and flip, whatever the primary is parked on — a call the archive closes, a `wait_for_agents` on children the archive terminates, or nothing at all (`retries_exhausted`). **Wake semantics:** it wakes an idle session exactly as user.message does (status_running + a queued turn) and joins the mid-turn chaining set; the rendering is ours (the reference's agent-facing prompt is unobservable) — a user-role message of the description plus an inline text rubric, or a file rubric's description alone, each closing with a line naming the deliverables directory `/mnt/session/outputs/`. How the reference conveys that directory, and whether in this message, is unobserved. **initial_events:** a non-empty list creates the session directly in `running` (documented) with `session.status_running` and the primary's `session.thread_status_running` written ahead of the initial events, which its first turn consumes — processing order, as a recorded deployment run's list opens (#793; "Session threads — the primary thread …", above); the body bound is the platform's global 4 MiB / 413, stricter than the documented 32 MB. **Projection timing:** the entry is born `pending` and `completed_at` stays null until a terminal result — a field the SDK types as required, whose pre-terminal value no source shows. *Evidence: docs/plan/21_outcomes.md (the SDK pins and documented rules it quotes, 2026-08-02); the text-rubric cap and the end event's required fields (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsTextRubricParams.Content and BetaManagedAgentsSpanOutcomeEvaluationEndEvent) and the required `completed_at` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsOutcomeEvaluationResource.CompletedAt); the reasoning cut from this row survives as code comments in internal/events/{inbound,outcomes}.go, internal/brain/replay.go, internal/api/sessions.go, internal/api/events.go (the wake trigger and `snapshotRubrics`, whose comment holds the snapshot rationale) and internal/domain/outcome.go; shapes pinned by internal/api/outcomes_test.go (TestDefineOutcomeTextRubricCap, TestInterruptMidGradingReferencesStart, TestCreateSessionInitialEvents, and the archive's TestArchivingASessionParkedMidOutcomeInterruptsTheOutcome, TestArchivingASessionParkedOnAConfirmationMidOutcomeInterruptsTheOutcome, TestArchivingACoordinatorWaitingOnAGatedChildInterruptsTheOutcome, TestArchivingASessionIdleOnRetriesExhaustedInterruptsTheOutcome), internal/api/events_test.go TestSendValidationSweep and internal/brain/replay_test.go TestBuildRequestRendersDefineOutcome* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the reference's rejection messages and rubric bounds, its agent-facing rendering and where it names the deliverables directory, and its pre-terminal `completed_at`).*
- **The outcome grader — everything the reference keeps opaque (plan 21 slices 3–4)** — The docs pin the loop's observable shape only; each choice below is ours, INFERRED, pending a recording. **Call**: one request on the session's own resolved model config (the reference names no grader model); system = our grader charge + the rubric (a file rubric from its acceptance snapshot; no snapshot or no object store grades the description alone, with an inline note); user = the description + a `# Deliverables` section (cloud only) + a role-labeled plain-text transcript; no tools, no previews, no thinking. Bounds ours: 4,000 chars/item, 200,000/transcript, 20,000/persisted explanation, 64 KiB of inlined deliverables. **Verdict protocol**: the reply must end `VERDICT: satisfied|needs_revision|failed`; one without it reads as needs_revision carrying the whole text (the budget still ends the loop). **Entry lifecycle**: a turn claimed on a `pending` entry flips it to `running` — the SDK's pending/running boundary read as claim time; entry state only, no wire event, and when the reference flips is unrecorded. **Trigger**: only a successful tool-less turn grades — a `requires_action` idle (HITL) or a `retries_exhausted` fault settles unevaluated and the outcome resumes on the next wake (the docs tie grading to the agent believing it has finished; the non-completion settlements are our reading). **Ordering ours**: grading is two-phase through the queue, so the committed start precedes the grader call — by queue latency, and on cloud by the deliverables harvest between them; heartbeats every 30s (cadence ours), never after the cycle's end event; an interrupt mid-grade wins and its verdict is discarded (the interrupt-settlement entry above). **Settlement**: satisfied/failed idle with plain `end_turn` (pending input chains a turn first); needs_revision returns the entry to `running` with `iteration+1`; a final-cycle needs_revision is reported `max_iterations_reached` (boundary reading: up to max_iterations cycles total) and runs the docs' one acknowledgment turn — both prompts replay-derived from the end event, no extra persisted event; a grader call that errors renders no end event at all, reverts the entry to `running`, and settles like a failed model turn (session.error + `retries_exhausted`, no automatic retry). **Usage**: the grader call's usage rides the end event and folds into the session's cumulative usage (whether the reference bills it is unrecorded). **Deliverables (slice 4)**: the docs show the grader scoring deliverables but never how they reach it, so the section's existence, format, inline rule and budget are ours; a storage-less deploy lists without contents, and self_hosted grades transcript-only. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/define-outcomes (fetched 2026-08-02); internal/brain/grader.go — its constants and doc comments hold every derivation cut from this row; internal/brain/brain.go (the pending→running flip), internal/events/span.go Heartbeat (the evaluating fence), internal/brain/replay.go (end-event injection), docs/ARCHITECTURE.md § "Outcome grading" (two-phase scheduling, the cloud harvest chain); internal/brain/grader_test.go TestOutcome{Satisfied,NeedsRevisionThenSatisfied,MaxIterationsReached,GraderError,InterruptDuringGrading,PendingFlipsRunningAtTurnStart,FileRubricSnapshot,GraderSeesDeliverables,GraderDeliverablesBudgetIsGreedy}* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the reference's grader model and prompt, when it flips an entry from pending to running, whether it bills the grader call's usage, and how deliverables reach the grader).*
- **The outputs harvest — a session's deliverables into the files registry (plan 21 slice 4, plan 38)** — Docs give the `/mnt/session/outputs/` directory, the session-scoped Files-API fetch, and the sentence that deliverables appear "shortly after the agent finishes writing them, sometimes a few seconds after the session goes idle"; the rest is ours, pending a recording, bar shapes the SDK pins. **When**: two triggers, both cloud-only. A grading cycle's — a session's `end_turn` with an active outcome enqueues an internal `outputs_harvest` in place of the grading turn, which that harvest chains on completion, so the grader reads a current snapshot. And the session's own idle, no outcome required (#263): a settlement that folds the whole session idle enqueues one, which is the docs' sentence read literally. Five do it — a tool-less `end_turn`, a `retries_exhausted` fault, a `requires_action` confirmation gate, a delegated turn's park, cut chain or gated ending, and a delegation-bound claim refusal (`cutExhaustedRun`) — because the docs distinguish no stop reason. An idle harvest attaches to a live sandbox only, never provisioning one — a plain session that ran no tool, or one whose sandbox was reaped, has nothing to attach to and settles a no-op; with no sandbox there is no walk and so no whole-snapshot replace, so a sandbox-lost session keeps whatever an earlier harvest published rather than having it deleted (a grading harvest still provisions, since the grader needs a current snapshot). A transient attach fault (anything but `ErrNotFound`) is propagated so the lease reclaim retries it, never taken for no-sandbox; and when a grading cycle takes over an idle-tagged item that walked no sandbox, the settlement requeues it as a grading harvest rather than grade a stale snapshot (it provisions on its next claim). Reusing the one `outputs_harvest` kind, the idle trigger is not rolling-deploy-safe — an old executor claiming a new idle item drains it unread, self-healing on the session's next idle (#587). The two settlements that *end* an outcome cycle (a verdict, a grader error) fold a session idle too and deliberately do not re-harvest: that cycle opened with a harvest of the same tree and only a tool-less grader call ran since, so a second pass would republish identical bytes under fresh `file_` ids. Reference timing (continuous, per-cycle, per-idle or at session end) stays unobserved, and so does whether the reference re-harvests on a session that idles repeatedly. **Walk**: a sorted bash listing of the regular files under it — dotfiles in, symlinks and directories out, a missing directory an empty snapshot; an absolute, unclean, escaping or over-1024-byte path is excluded and logged, and each segment must pass the upload endpoint's own filename rule, so no harvested `filename` is one this platform's upload would 400 — but where an upload keeps only a name's final path component, the harvest keeps the whole `/`-joined path under that 1024-byte cap, the deliberate laxity. Past the exec output cap the listing degrades to its complete-entry sorted prefix, logged, never a fault. **Caps** (ours, greedy in path order; the reference documents none): 50 MiB per file, 200 files and 500 MiB per session — an over-cap or ineligible file is absent from the registry, never an error; a tree always yields the same snapshot. **Rows**: one per path — `filename` is the sandbox-relative path, `downloadable:true` (the seam 0008 reserved), mime from the extension alone via a pinned in-code table, never the host's (#264), `application/octet-stream` unknown; `scope` is the SDK's own `{id, type:"session"}`, so what is unobserved is whether the reference names, scopes or exposes harvested files this way, not the field. Rows list in unfiltered `GET /v1/files` too and download byte-identically. **Snapshot**: each harvest that read the sandbox replaces the session's rows whole — delete-all + insert-all in one transaction, fresh `file_` ids per cycle (plan 21 said upsert), vanished paths dropped, the replaced snapshot's object keys enqueued for the control plane's drain inside the publishing transaction (#703); bytes stage to their final keys first, so a fault leaves the previous snapshot intact and only orphans behind. **Fence**: that transaction reads the session's live outcome state first and the item's own trigger second. An outcome still `evaluating` owns the pass whichever trigger enqueued it — the two flavors share one live item per session, so a cycle beginning while an idle-triggered item is still queued has its own enqueue swallowed by the dedup key, and refusing to serve it would strand that outcome in `evaluating` forever — served, it publishes and chains the grader when it walked a live sandbox, or requeues itself as a grading harvest when it found none, so the grader never reads a snapshot this pass did not freshly collect. Once nothing is evaluating, only the trigger separates a grading cycle that a `user.interrupt` settled mid-harvest (discard — nothing publishes, the item completes, staged bytes are discarded) from a session that never had an outcome at all (publish, chaining nothing). The trigger rides the work item's existing `metadata` jsonb rather than a second kind, so neither the schema nor the executor's dispatch grew. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/define-outcomes, and managed-agents/files § "Listing and downloading session files" for the idle sentence (fetched 2026-09-03); internal/executor/harvest.go, whose comments now carry the derivations cut from here (listing script, path validation, truncation, caps, the two-flavor fence) and internal/executor/executor.go sessionForRun's idle carve-out; internal/api/filesupload.go validateFilename; internal/mimetab (#264); internal/store/migrations/0017_outputs_harvest.sql (files_scope_filename_idx); internal/brain/grader.go settleEndTurn and enqueueIdleHarvest; internal/queue/queue.go EnqueueOutputsHarvest, RequeueAsGrading and Item.ChainGrading; docs/plan/38_idle-outputs-harvest.md; internal/executor/harvest_test.go (TestIdleHarvestPublishesSnapshotWithoutChainingATurn, TestFreshOutcomeGradingReclaimsAStaleIdleHarvestSlot, TestNoLiveSandboxGradingCollisionRequeuesInsteadOfGradingStale, TestIdleHarvestDeadSessionDrains); executor_integration_test.go TestHarvestRealSandbox / TestHarvestTruncatedListingRealSandbox; BetaFileMetadata.Scope is a BetaFileScope whose Type is constant.Session, default "session", beside Downloadable and the scope_id query param (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata.Scope and BetaFileScope.Type and BetaFileMetadata.Downloadable and BetaFileListParams.ScopeID)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle the harvest's timing and caps, whether it re-harvests on every idle, and how the reference exposes harvested files); landed for #77.*
- **The idle harvest trigger — five readings the docs' one sentence does not settle (plan 38)** — "Sometimes a few seconds after the session goes idle" is best-effort language about *when* files appear, not a specification, and the entry above records what this platform built on it. Five readings inside it are ours. **Which idles count**: every *brain* settlement fold outside an outcome cycle, whatever stop reason it carries — five of them: a tool-less `end_turn`, a `retries_exhausted` fault, a `requires_action` confirmation gate, a delegated turn's park, cut chain or gated ending, and a delegation-bound claim refusal. Whether the reference harvests only after a clean `end_turn` is unobserved; the docs distinguish no reason, so neither does this. Four idle folds are the deliberate exceptions, not oversights: an outcome cycle's own two — its verdict and its grader error — for the reason the entry above gives, and the two the *API* makes rather than the brain — a `user.interrupt` and the archive of a session's last running thread — a separate subsystem tracked by #586 (the interrupt also poses its own publish-vs-discard question). So the honest scope is every brain settlement fold, not every session-level idle fold whatever produced it. **Repeat idles**: a session that idles several times re-harvests each time, through the same delete-all/insert-all snapshot replace a multi-cycle grading session already gets. Whether the reference re-harvests, and with what semantics, is unobserved. **Child threads**: a multi-agent session harvests only at true session-level quiescence — a child idling under a still-running sibling, or a coordinator parking under live children, harvests nothing — mirroring the rule grading already follows. The reference exposes no thread-level file scope to compare against. **self_hosted**: excluded, for the reason its own entry above gives. **Latency**: nothing here promises the docs' "few seconds". The item is queued inside the settlement's transaction and picked up on the executor's own poll cadence, so the delay is a deployment's queue latency plus the sandbox walk, and no bound is claimed. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/files § "Listing and downloading session files" (fetched 2026-09-03); docs/plan/38_idle-outputs-harvest.md § 2 ("What no source pins") and decisions 4 and 6; internal/brain/grader.go enqueueIdleHarvest, whose comment carries the gate's derivation; internal/brain/thread_test.go TestNoHarvestUntilTheSessionItselfIdles, internal/brain/delegate_test.go TestParkedCoordinatorWithLiveChildrenSkipsHarvest, internal/brain/brain_test.go TestRetriesExhaustedIdleEnqueuesOutputsHarvest / TestPermissionAskIdleEnqueuesOutputsHarvest, internal/executor/harvest_test.go TestReHarvestReplacesSnapshotPerPath* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle which idles the reference harvests on, whether it re-harvests on each one, and whether a multi-agent session harvests per thread or only at session quiescence).*
- **MCP client transport — Streamable HTTP only, no HTTP+SSE fallback (plan 29 slice 2)** — `internal/mcp` speaks Streamable HTTP and nothing else. What is documented: the 2026-07-28 spec classifies the separate HTTP+SSE transport of 2024-11-05 as **Deprecated** — "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it … It is eligible for removal in a future revision" — while also giving clients that want to reach older servers an exact detection procedure ("If it fails with HTTP status code `400 Bad Request`, `404 Not Found`, or `405 Method Not Allowed` **and** the response body is not a recognized modern JSON-RPC error … Issue a GET request to the server URL, expecting … an `endpoint` event as the first event"). Both halves are quoted here because they pull in opposite directions and the choice between them is ours: the fallback is split out of the client rather than shipped with it, so until it lands, a server that hosts *only* the deprecated endpoints is unreachable and surfaces as an ordinary connection failure.
