Nothing is silently degraded — the SDK negotiates every protocol version from 2024-11-05 up **over Streamable HTTP**, so an old server that hosts the modern endpoint is reached today, and only the transport-level fallback is outstanding. Two smaller readings ride along. The standalone SSE stream is **disabled** (`DisableStandaloneSSE`): 2026-07-28 removed the GET endpoint it used, and a per-work-item connection asks one question and closes, so it has no use for a server-initiated stream in any revision. Against a *modern* server the setting is a no-op — the go-sdk returns before opening the GET whenever the negotiated version is 2026-07-28 or later (checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/streamable.go streamableClientConn.sessionUpdated) — so what it actually governs is an older negotiation, where the spec makes the GET optional and a `tools/list` answer arrives on the POST regardless — which is what the contract suite exercises rather than a case it cannot reach, since go-sdk serves 2026-07-28 only from a stateless handler and every fixture therefore negotiates 2025-11-25; a test counts the standalone GETs. Whether the reference's client opens one is unobserved. And the client identifies itself to servers as `managed-agent-platform` with no version; the reference's client name is unobserved, and an operator reading their server logs sees ours. *Evidence: modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http § "Backward Compatibility" and § "HTTP+SSE Transport (2024-11-05)" (fetched 2026-08-08); internal/mcp/mcp.go (Connect); internal/mcp/mcp_test.go* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference's client opens a standalone SSE stream, and the client name it sends); the transport landed for #45, and the fallback itself is #348 — named by plan 29's architecture but by no slice, so it kept a home when #45 closed.*
- **MCP tool listing — what a malformed entry becomes, and the ceilings the protocol does not set (plan 29 slice 2)** — The reference makes its MCP connection server-side, so the exchange is invisible from the wire and only its outcomes are (`agent.mcp_tool_use`/`agent.mcp_tool_result`, and typed `mcp_connection_failed_error`/`mcp_authentication_failed_error`). Every reading here is therefore ours. An entry the platform cannot faithfully hand a model is **skipped**, never fatal to the listing — one bad entry must not deny an agent the rest of a catalog: an unusable name (empty, over 128 bytes, or outside `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, the SDK's own `validateToolName` charset rather than a guessed Anthropic rule), an `inputSchema` that is absent, null, not an object, or whose root `type` is not `"object"`, and a duplicate name (first wins — a malformed first entry leaves the name free, because the name is recorded after the schema check). A repeated pagination cursor **fails** the listing rather than looping; every cursor seen is remembered, not just the previous one. Five ceilings are platform policy and not protocol, which sets none: 100 pages, two minutes per listing, 30 seconds on any request the SDK detaches from the caller's context, 64 KiB on a single response's header block — the one a *legitimate* server can hit — and 8 MiB **accounted** across a connection, which is not the same as delivered: bodies count in full but header blocks only as far as they survive parsing to be counted, so the bytes actually on the wire can far exceed it (measured once at 91.8 MB against 3,900 bytes charged). Two readings are forced by the SDK's typed surface rather than chosen: a successful result carrying no `tools` field is recorded as "this server has no tools" rather than as malformed, and an empty-string cursor ends the listing — a real divergence from the pagination spec, which says an empty cursor is valid and MUST NOT be treated as the end, for a case no known server exercises. `"tools": [null]` panics the go-sdk (checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/streamable_headers.go validateParamHeaderAnnotations) and is recovered at the call site, because the executor is shared by every session on the host. *Evidence: internal/mcp/mcp.go — ListTools, listPage, usableName, inputSchema, limitedTransport, headerBytes, and the constants ListTimeout, maxToolPages and maxHeaderBytesPerResponse, each carrying its own rationale, its measured boundary, and the record of the ceilings this entry published and had falsified (MaxResponseBytes's doc comment, including the rule that came out of it: do not multiply by a quantity nothing bounds); the detached-context close the 30-second bound covers (checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — internal/xcontext/xcontext.go Detach; checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/streamable.go StreamableClientTransport.Connect and streamableClientConn.Close); internal/mcp/mcp_test.go drives every boundary from both sides; MCP 2026-07-28 server/utilities/pagination; checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/tool.go validateToolName; checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/server.go Server.AddTool; checked against go-sdk v1.7.0 — mcp/protocol.go ListToolsResult.Tools and ListToolsResult.NextCursor.* *Tracked: #78 (pointing a reference session at a server with a malformed entry, or a repeated cursor, would settle whether it skips or fails the listing; an over-paginating or header-heavy server would settle what ceilings it sets where the protocol sets none); landed for #45.*
- **MCP-client egress and the tool catalog — what the reference leaves unobserved (plan 29 slice 2)** — The reference pins four things (that `limited.allow_mcp_servers`, default false, additively admits "MCP server endpoints configured on the agent"; that networking exists on **cloud** environments only; that session creation validates no MCP connectivity; and that a failed connection is "retried on the next `session.status_idle` to `session.status_running` transition") and leaves the rest unobservable, since its MCP exchange is server-side. **Where the policy is enforced is ours**: the platform dials from the executor's own process, structurally outside the per-session gate, so `limited` binds at that dial or nowhere — and before it, never after, since a refusal that still opened the connection would already have told the server the session exists. Reading a documented *sandbox-egress* field as also governing the platform's own dial is our reading, and the one that gives it something to admit. **The policy's type decides, not the environment kind**: a `self_hosted` config carrying a networking block is one the REST surface cannot have written, so it came from an import or a hand-written UPDATE and is a policy somebody meant; an unrecognized type **refuses**, matching `gate.newPolicy`'s fail-closed direction, because the schema constrains `config->>'type'` but leaves `config->'networking'` alone and a config-preserving update carries a stored value forward unvalidated. A block naming no type reads as no policy — the one corner this cannot distinguish, since `domain.Networking` is a value struct — and it adds no reach a hand-written `unrestricted` would not. **The catalog** is a per-session snapshot keyed `(session_id, server_name)` — `(session_id, thread_id, server_name)` since plan 35 slice 3, each thread discovering its own agent's servers — rather than a URL-keyed cache, because two sessions may reach one server with different credentials and see different tools. Its row is the retry state, and the three states are ours: absent means never reached, `failed` is re-attempted next work cycle, `ready` is offered and not re-fetched. **Nothing disables a server for the rest of a session** — no source documents such a state. A `ready` row is matched on url as well as name, and rows an agent patch no longer declares are deleted in the patch's own transaction; the url comparison and a settle-time re-read under the session's row lock are deliberate redundancy against a missed or overtaken delete. **A server that cannot be reached is a row, not a fault** — an unreachable endpoint, a non-http(s) url, a policy refusal, and a server the pass's time budget did not reach all land as `failed` and the turn chains anyway, since faulting would reclaim-loop against a server that is simply down. Failures surface as `mcp_connection_failed_error`, or `mcp_authentication_failed_error` where a credential was refused (the connection worked well enough to be refused), **once per work cycle** rather than once per turn — a cadence that falls out of the row rather than being imposed. Two failed rows say nothing: one the pass timed out on, and one whose url is not http(s), since nothing was dialled. **What a row may hold is bounded by this platform, not the protocol**: a reason at 2,000 bytes, a listing at a quarter-megabyte with tools past it dropped whole. Slice 6b made the dials concurrent — declaration order never was a fact about a server, and serially one slow server kept those behind it out of the catalog for the session's life — while credential resolution stays serial, because an OAuth refresh run twice on one token can make an issuer revoke the family. The pass still runs on the goroutine that takes a host's work items, now for one server's dial rather than every server's, bounded by `EXECUTOR_MCP_PASS_TIMEOUT`. *Evidence: internal/executor/mcpwork.go carries each rule beside the code it governs (mcpEgressAllowed's type-not-kind derivation, the settle-time re-read, the row-state machine, the concurrency split and why credentials stay serial); internal/store/migrations for the mcp_catalogs shape; internal/api/sessions.go for the patch-time delete (and internal/api/events.go for the idle→running clear of failed rows); the named tests in internal/executor/mcpwork_test.go and internal/api/mcptoolset_test.go pin the states and cadences.* **Both questions are now recorded, and the first one's mechanism is not what this entry assumed.** `allow_mcp_servers` is a **create-time admission check**, not a dial-time guard: a session whose agent declares an MCP server, created on an environment with `{"type":"limited","allowed_hosts":[],"allow_mcp_servers":false}`, is refused with a **400** and never exists — `MCP server host(s) blocked by environment network policy: "deepwiki" (mcp.deepwiki.com). Add these hosts to the environment's allowed_hosts, or set allow_mcp_servers=true.` The check extracts the host from each `mcp_servers[].url` and cross-validates it against the environment's policy, and both paths its message names do work: `allowed_hosts:["mcp.deepwiki.com"]` and `["*.deepwiki.com"]` each admit the session with the flag still false. It does **not** apply to `self_hosted`, whose config carries no `networking` block. This platform gates the dial instead (`mcpEgressAllowed`, `egressRefusal`), so a misconfigured environment here is a running session emitting an error event per cycle where the reference is a synchronous 400 the caller cannot miss; closing that is #571, which keeps the dial-time check because `mcp_servers` is mid-session-mutable. The second question settles as this entry read it: a failed server is **never** disabled, and the unit of its `session.error` is the **idle→running work cycle**, not the message — five messages across four cycles produced four errors, one cycle absorbing two messages — with the turn still reaching `end_turn`. Two incidentals came free: the error and the status change share a timestamp exactly on every cycle but the first, whose create path differs, and input arriving while a cycle is open **joins that cycle** rather than queueing a fresh turn. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `sess.create.mcp-host-exact-gate`, `sess.create.mcp-host-wildcard-gate` and `session.events.list.dead-five-turns`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §D.* *Tracked: #571 (validate declared MCP hosts at session create); landed for #45.*

- **Answering an MCP call — the execution locus, the result's blocks, and a failure's two halves (plan 29 slice 4)** — The reference's MCP exchange is server-side, so only the event pair is observable and everything between the two events is ours. **Locus**: the platform's own executor answers every MCP call on **every** environment kind, `self_hosted` included, because a BYOC worker's contract has no MCP surface — which makes MCP's precedence over the built-ins a correctness rule rather than a preference, applied at all six settlement sites. **A tool that ran and failed is a result, not a fault**: MCP asks servers to report it as an ordinary `is_error` result so the model can self-correct, and making it a work-item failure would reclaim-loop against a tool that fails identically every time. **A server that refuses is not a connection that failed** — an unknown tool, an `input_required` answer, unusable blocks, or a value that will not re-marshal all arrive over a working connection; the client marks server-answered errors (`ErrServerAnswered`) as a boundary rule rather than a list of cases. A JSON-RPC error is one of those answers only when the operation's most recent exchange was a **2xx** that this platform's response limit let through (#641): a non-2xx on the call is the HTTP layer failing whatever its body says, so it produces both events below. That exchange is not always the call's own — a reply the client posts to the server's mid-call request counts too — so a reply refused transiently turns the tool's genuine refusal into an extra `mcp_connection_failed_error`. The trade is ours and deliberate, and the reference cannot show its own because its exchange is server-side: reading only the call's exchange would instead report nothing when a refused reply ends the connection. **A call that never reached its server produces both events**: an `is_error` result so the turn continues and replay stays valid, plus a `session.error` naming the server so an operator sees a connection to heal. Its `retry_status` is **always `retrying`** — plan 29 specified `terminal` for an egress block and that is corrected here, because the union describes the *session* and this session neither terminates nor flushes; an operator can also widen `allowed_hosts` mid-session and heal the refusal. Messages are cut to `scheme://host`, since an `mcp_servers` entry may carry a credential. **Content blocks**: MCP's five types and the event's four do not correspond, so text and image translate, an embedded resource becomes a `document` titled with its URI, a resource link becomes text (never a url source, which would have the model's side dial an address this platform never vetted), audio is described rather than dropped, anything else is dropped, and an answer rendering to nothing gets one text block saying so. What a block may declare is the **intersection** of the event schema and the Messages schema the brain replays into — a block this platform can store but never send again fails every later turn on a log that cannot take it back. **Budgets are ours**, neither MCP nor the reference setting either: 100 KiB (`toolset.MaxOutputBytes`) charged per marshalled block, kept or dropped whole, with a trailing block naming what went and one exemption for a block whose text was already capped; and two minutes (`toolset.DefaultTimeout`) bounding the whole call, not one request. The execution pass is bounded as a whole too, by `EXECUTOR_MCP_PASS_TIMEOUT` (five minutes, operator-settable through Helm and compose) — checked *between* calls and never cancelling one, so its first call is always made; the reference documents no such limit either. No at-most-once guarantee is offered, because MCP has no idempotency key and the reference documents none. *Evidence: internal/executor/mcpexec.go and internal/mcp/calltool.go carry the reasoning beside the code; the claims above are each pinned by a named test in internal/executor/mcpexec_test.go, mcpwork_test.go, internal/mcp/calltool_test.go and internal/api/confirmation_test.go — the test names state the rules (TestMCPToolFailureIsTheModelsToRead, TestMCPProtocolFailureIsNotReportedAsAConnectionFailure, TestMCPBlobResourceRidesOnlyABlockTheWireCanCarry, TestMCPExemptionFollowsTheCappedBlockNotThePosition, TestToolResultResumeSchedulesAnOutstandingMCPCall, and the rest); both schemas, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentMCPToolResultEvent and BetaManagedAgentsAgentMCPToolResultEventContentUnion, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamessage.go BetaToolResultBlockParam and BetaToolResultBlockParamContentUnion; modelcontextprotocol.io § tools for is_error's rationale.* **Two of the three questions are settled, and this platform matches on both.** The locus is the platform, inline: a recorded call runs `user.message | model_request_start | thinking | agent.mcp_tool_use | model_request_end | agent.mcp_tool_result | model_request_start | agent.message | model_request_end | idle{end_turn}`, so the result lands **between one model request and the next with no idle, no work item and no executor round trip** — against a `bash` call in the same wave whose result arrives only after `session.status_idle` and a confirmation. It holds **without a worker**: a session on a `self_hosted` environment with none running was served all the same, because the platform dials from its own side. `agent.mcp_tool_result`'s key set is `content, id, is_error, mcp_tool_use_id, processed_at, type` — `mcp_tool_use_id`, not `tool_use_id`, which is what this platform already emits — and an MCP **text** block maps to a `text` content block **verbatim**, unwrapped, the server's own JSON arriving as the text of one block. `retry_status` on a failed dial is `retrying`, as the connection-failure entry reads it. What stays unobserved is narrower than the entry opened with: the embedded-resource, `resource_link`, audio and empty-content-array cases, each of which needs a server whose tool returns one, and what the reference reports for a call answered with a non-2xx. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `session.events.list.mcp-real-call` and `session.events.list.mcp-self-hosted-locus`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §D.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording against a server returning an embedded resource, a resource_link, audio, or an empty content array would settle how each renders, and one answering the call with a non-2xx how that is reported); landed for #45, #641.*

- **Offering an MCP tool to the model — the name it is given, the ways it can be lost, and the turn that waits (plan 29 slice 4b)** — **The reference composes the name it shows the model and decomposes it on the event it commits**: a recorded session named its own tools `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` and siblings, while `agent.mcp_tool_use` carries the bare `name` beside `mcp_server_name`. So `mcp__{server}__{tool}` is the reference's shape as much as ours, and the composition this entry was opened to record as a divergence is not one — the detail is in the recording paragraph below. No *source* documents the convention (`mcp__` appears in neither the SDK nor the `ant` CLI), which is why it was inferred the other way for as long as it was. What is genuinely ours is the *necessity*: this brain assembles one flat `tools[]` for an ordinary Messages endpoint, which carries a name and nothing else, so here the composed name is the only thing distinguishing two servers that both offer `search` — without it a call would be unattributable — where the reference has a field to fall back on. It is confined to the provider request — the committed event carries the bare name and `mcp_server_name` as the SDK types them, and replay reassembles the pair so every later turn re-sends a name that turn actually offered. The composed name must fit `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$` **here**, that being the Messages endpoint's constraint and not the reference's — the recording shows three composed names of 69, 75 and 76 characters offered and echoed back verbatim, with no truncation and no drop — while the mcp-connector page documents a server name of 1–255 characters and an `mcp_toolset` `configs[]` name of 1–128, both Anthropic's bounds, `configs[]` having no existence in the MCP protocol at all. So a pair legal on both wires can compose into one *this* endpoint rejects — and it would reject the whole request, every turn, forever. Bytes outside the class are **mangled to `_` one-for-one** rather than dropped, so the length checked before composition is the length produced. **Five ways a declared tool goes unoffered, each costing only its own tool and each a log line**: a name too long to compose; a name already taken (the agent's own `tools[]` wins over a third party's, and flattening is not injective, so two servers or two mangled names can collide — declaration order settles all three here, where the reference instead reserves the `mcp__` prefix at agent create and so makes the first of the three impossible by construction); a server whose listing failed or has not happened; a `configs[]` entry naming a tool the server does not report (the only one the docs settle — it warns without erroring); and a definition past the 256 KiB of MCP definitions one request carries. Two further drops are **silent by design** — a nameless tool and a repeated name are already refused where a listing is read, so warning about them would be noise about a state that cannot arise. The reports are log lines, not events, bounded at sixteen per turn; plan 29 proposed a `system.message` and that is corrected here, since the event is client-sent and replays into the system prompt, which one per turn would grow without bound. **A turn waits for a listing it has never had** (no model call; the item is handed back and the discovery driver chains the turn), while a *failed* row is an answer rather than an absence — the turn runs without that server. Failed rows are deleted on an idle→running transition, matching the reference's documented "retried on the next transition" cadence. An outstanding call is dialled only where its listing was read and only while the agent still declares the server there, since `mcp_servers` is mid-session-mutable and dialling a new address would ask a different party a question it never advertised. **A name the model was not offered is answered inline**: it still commits as an ordinary `agent.tool_use` (`evaluated_permission: "deny"`, `name`, `input`, `session_thread_id`) — `deny`, not `allow`, because a `self_hosted` worker built to the reference contract streams `agent.tool_use` directly and may own this very name in its own registered toolset regardless of what this agent's config disabled, and does not treat this platform's own `agent.tool_result` as answering it (it dedups only on `user.tool_result`/`user.custom_tool_result`) — an `allow` stamp would tell such a worker to run what the settlement already refused. The settlement answers it in the same commit with an `is_error` `agent.tool_result` reading `unknown tool %q: not offered to this agent` (the fully rendered text bounded to 16 KiB by `unknownToolAnswer`, which quotes the name exactly rather than trimming it as `delegationText` does) — both landing on the calling thread's own log, exactly as a delegation call and its answer do — corrected from treating it as client-executed, which parked the call as `agent.custom_tool_use` for a client that had declared no such tool and would never post a result, stranding the session `running` forever with no stop reason and no way for an operator to see why. The turn chains to the model's next request only when nothing else in it remains outstanding: mixed into the same turn as a gated `tool_exec` call or an `always_ask` one, it is answered all the same but the turn still waits on those, precisely as it would without it. A run of consecutive turns this settlement alone answers and that wake no other thread — unoffered names, genuine delegation calls, wrong-role calls to the other half, or any mix; a turn whose answer wakes a thread resets the count, as `chain && !woke` in `commitDelegatedTurn` says — is capped at the same per-thread `maxSettlementChain` (25) every shape already bounds, and the cap's own `delegation_chain_exhausted_error` fires the same way and reads the same kind-agnostic message regardless of the mix that built it: a run with no genuine call in it is cut exactly as one with only genuine calls is, so a coordinator parked on a child it capped is told and woken either way. What stays genuine-only is the session-wide delegation budget: it counts, and this run only spends, a turn that held at least one call the model was actually offered (#567). Declared custom calls use the shared external-wait processor described above (#375). `session.error` messages are bounded, since every string one is built from is capped nowhere it is stored. The agent's own tools are laid down before the MCP ones, which makes the collision rule decidable in one pass; ordering within `tools[]` is unobservable on the reference. An MCP call gates as an ordinary ask tool (`DefaultMCPToolsetPolicy` is `always_ask`), and work follows the first unresolved call on each thread. Across independently runnable threads the shared queue chooses `mcp_exec`, then `web_exec`, then `tool_exec`; this priority cannot pass an earlier call on the same thread. *Evidence: internal/brain/mcptools.go, internal/brain/replay.go, internal/brain/delegate.go (d.run's unoffered branch, answering the same way a delegation call is) and internal/toolset/mcp.go carry the reasoning beside the code; roughly thirty named tests pin the rules individually across internal/brain/mcptools_test.go, mcpturn_test.go, internal/api/mcptoolset_test.go, internal/executor/mcpexec_test.go and internal/toolset/mcp_test.go — the names state the claims (TestTwoToolNamesThatSanitizeAlikeContestOneName, TestAFailedListingRunsTheTurnWithoutThatServer, TestWakingASessionRetriesItsFailedMCPListings, TestMCPCallForARepointedServerIsNotDialledElsewhere, and the rest); platform.claude.com tool-use "Define tools" for the name regex, the mcp-connector guides for the committed event's bare-name shape and the two bounds; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentMCPToolUseEvent and BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolUseEvent and BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolResultEvent, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamessage.go BetaToolParam.* **The recording is what settled the composition question above, and the detail is worth keeping.** Asked to name its tools verbatim, a recorded session on `https://mcp.deepwiki.com/mcp` answered `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question`, `mcp__deepwiki__read_wiki_contents`, `mcp__deepwiki__read_wiki_structure` — **the name the reference shows the model is composed, not bare.** Both halves hold at once: the model sees `mcp__{server}__{tool}` while `agent.mcp_tool_use` carries the bare `name` beside `mcp_server_name`, so the reference composes outbound and decomposes inbound, and this platform's composition was never a divergence at all. Two consequences. The reference **reserves the prefix**: a `custom` tool named `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` is refused at agent create with `tools.1.custom.name: custom tool name "…" uses reserved MCP tool prefix`, which makes a collision impossible by construction rather than settled by declaration order as it is here. And there is **no 64-character ceiling**: a 50-character server name produced composed names of 69, 75 and 76 characters — all three echoed back intact, with no truncation, no drop and a turn that ran normally, where `mcpNameFits` would not have offered the tool at all — the bound is the Messages tool-use guide's `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$`, a constraint of the endpoint this brain assembles a flat `tools[]` for rather than of the reference's own protocol, so what the recording shows is that the reference is not subject to it. Both are #573. The waiting question settles as assumed: a session created with `initial_events`, its first turn due before any listing could have happened, shows a **single** `span.model_request_start` with the MCP tools already known — the turn waits for the listing before the first model call. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `session.events.list.mcp-tool-names`, `session.events.list.long` and `agent.create.mcp-name-collision`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §D.* *Tracked: #573 (reserve the `mcp__` prefix at agent create; decide whether the 64-byte cap can be lifted or has to stay a property of the endpoint this brain assembles for), #78 (what the reference does with a tool name it cannot offer, the half of this question the recording did not reach — the listing-wait half it did); landed for #45.*

- **The console-API dialect's edges — rejections, paging bounds and the reserved org segment (plan 30 slice 2)** — The 2026-08-10 capture of the reference console showed the happy paths only: a create, a list, a revoke, and their bodies. Every edge our implementation answers is therefore a local choice, recorded here rather than presented as mirrored. **Rejections:** issuing on a `cloud` environment is a 400 (its work is consumed by this platform's own in-process executor, which holds no environment key, so the credential would authorize a worker that does not exist — and the reference offers no key UI for its cloud environments either, which is suggestive but not a captured response); issuing on an archived environment is a 400; an unknown or malformed environment id is a 404 in the same shape, so the namespace is no better an enumeration oracle than `/v1`; an unknown, malformed or *foreign* `token_id` all take one 404 branch, so revocation can neither reach across environments nor confirm that an id exists elsewhere. **The `name` bound** is 1–128 characters after trimming, and a whitespace-only name is rejected; the dialect's own bound is unobserved, and 128 is a local pick sized to a hostname-or-phrase label. **Paging** is offset / `total` / `has_more`, matching the observed listing envelope rather than this platform's wire-side keyset `next_page` cursor — the observed listing reported `limit: 100`, so 100 is both our default and our cap, and a `limit` or `offset` outside its range is a 400 whose message is ours. Offset paging can repeat or skip a row across concurrent writes where a keyset cursor cannot; an operator's per-host key list is small enough that it never surfaces, and matching the dialect wins. **The `{organization_id}` segment** exists because the reference's does and because org/workspace/project are this platform's reserved tenancy keys (principle 5); v1 answers only for `"default"` and 404s anything else *before* the environment lookup, naming the organization in the message. Whether the reference validates that segment at all, and what it says when it does not match, is unobserved. Confirming any of this needs a capture of the reference console driving its own error paths, which the happy-path session deliberately did not provoke. *Evidence: internal/api/consoleapi.go; internal/api/consoleapi_test.go (TestConsoleKeyIssueRejectsBadRequests, TestConsoleKeyListPagesAndRendersNullExpiry, TestConsoleKeyRevokeRejectsIdsItDoesNotOwn, TestConsoleKeyRoutesRejectOtherOrganizations); docs/plan/30_environment-keys-console-issuance.md § "The console-API surface"* *Tracked: #78 (a capture of the reference console driving its own error paths would settle these rejections, the dialect's own `name` bound, and whether it validates the `{organization_id}` segment).*
- **`self` recursion versus the depth-1 rule** — The reference admits at most one `{"type":"self"}` roster entry, which it says allows "recursive self-invocation", while its depth rule is phrased as a property of the agents a roster references — they "must not themselves have `multiagent` set" — and `self` resolves to an agent that does. The docs add only that session-level overrides "apply to the coordinator and its `self` copies", saying nothing about whether a copy inherits the roster. If it does, depth 1 does not bind the one topology that can recurse. Ours is unaffected either way, and by a different mechanism than the rule: `resolveRoster` exempts `self` from the depth check (read literally the rule would forbid the documented feature), but the six delegation tools are injected **by role**, so a child thread is never offered `create_agent` whatever its own snapshot carries — a `self` copy is a child and cannot spawn. So the inference is about the reference alone, and it matters because a reference that does let a `self` copy recurse bounds depth only by its 25-thread cap. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents multiagent-orchestration (the `self` entry, "recursive self-invocation", the depth sentence, and the session-override sentence); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsMultiagentParams.Agents; internal/api/roster.go (the documented `self` exemption); internal/brain/mcptools.go (resolveTools, hasRoster)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of a session whose roster names `self`, read back from a spawned copy's own agent snapshot, would settle whether the reference lets that copy spawn in turn).*
- **Session ID prefix parsing (all endpoints accepting a session id)** — Canonical prefix is `sesn_`; the alternate `session_` form is accepted on input and normalized to `sesn_` before lookup. Whether the reference **accepts** that spelling is unobserved: every concrete session id in the SDK and the `ant` CLI, all of them test fixtures (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession_test.go TestBetaSessionGetWithOptionalParams; checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betasession_test.go TestBetaSessionsRetrieve), and on the public sessions pages is `sesn_`, and the only reference-side `session_` anywhere is the work-data schema's description of its own `data.id`, which the SDK's worker feeds straight back into `/v1/sessions/{id}/…` — so if the reference ever emits that spelling it must accept it, and a lenient parse is the safe side of an unrecorded question. **The entry this one replaces asserted the accept as settled** — "mirroring the reference's dual form" — on evidence that was this repo's own wire-compatibility rule restated plus two history sections that no longer carry the sentence; it is corrected rather than deleted so the reversal stays auditable. *Evidence: BetaSessionWorkData.ID's description spells it `session_` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSessionWorkData.ID) against "a `sesn_...` value" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaManagedAgentsSessionActor.SessionID) and the SDK's own fixture, which emits `sesn_test` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller_test.go workJSONWithSecret); `sessionID := work.Data.ID`, fed straight into the per-session calls (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — worker.go EnvironmentWorker.handleItem); internal/domain/id.go (altSessionPrefix), internal/api/sessions.go (normalizeSessionID); platform.claude.com api/beta/sessions/retrieve, fetched 2026-08-23 (the Get Session example answers `sesn_011CZkZAtmR3yMPDzynEDxu7`, and the page documents no alternate spelling — its `session_id` path parameter is a field name, not a prefix)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference accepts a `session_` session id on input at all, and which spelling its work-poll `data.id` actually carries).*
- **Memory-store update — an explicit JSON null on `name`, `description` or `metadata` (plan 36 slice 1)** — The update body's schema admits null on all three fields and gives it no meaning, and the SDK's `omitzero` never sends one, so what the reference answers is unobserved. This platform reads each null by the rule its sibling resources already apply: `name` is required, so null — like `""` — is a 400 `name cannot be cleared` (the agent and environment updates); a null `description` clears it, exactly as the documented `""` does (the agent update's description); a null `metadata` bag preserves it (the shared patch). *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryStoreRequestBody`, each field `anyOf [T, null]`, no stated meaning (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryStoreRequestBody), and the SDK's `param.Opt` fields, omitted when unset (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreUpdateParams); internal/api/memorystores.go updateMemoryStore; internal/api/agents.go and environments.go (`cannot be cleared`); internal/api/memorystores_test.go TestMemoryStoreUpdateSemantics* *Tracked: #78 (not among the plan's fifteen items: a recording of `POST /v1/memory_stores/{id}` with `{"name": null}`, `{"description": null}` and `{"metadata": null}` would settle all three).*
- **Renaming a memory onto an ancestor or a descendant of another memory's path — the same 409 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The occupancy rule itself is spec-stated and mirrored verbatim: a create's path "must be unoccupied: if a memory already exists at the path, or the path is an ancestor or descendant of an existing memory's path, the request returns `memory_path_conflict_error` (HTTP 409)", and renaming "onto a path occupied by a different memory" returns the same error. What no source states is the third case — a *rename* onto an ancestor or a descendant, `/a/b` existing and `/notes.md` renamed to `/a`. This platform runs one predicate for both operations, so a rename answers exactly what a create at that path would, `conflicting_memory_id` and `conflicting_path` included. The predicate is a literal `left()` comparison and never `LIKE`: `_` and `%` are legal path bytes, and a pattern would make `/acb/x` occupy `/a_b`. *Evidence: the spec's BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams ("must be unoccupied…"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams, and BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams ("Rename never overwrites; delete or rename the blocking memory first"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams; internal/api/memories.go occupiedBy; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryPathOccupancy; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 4 — a rename onto an ancestor or a descendant of another memory's path: its status and error type).*
- **Content over 100 kB, or content that is not valid UTF-8, is a 400 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The reference documents the 100 kB (102,400-byte) ceiling as a property of the memory object — "Maximum 100 kB" on `content` — and never says what a request exceeding it answers. It has a 413 `request_too_large` family, which this platform mirrors for its own uploads (`errors.go`'s `errTypeRequestTooLarge`, used by the file and skill upload paths), so a 413 is entirely plausible here; the platform answers 400 instead because the reference frames the bound as validation of the memory rather than of the request. The reference's worker cannot tell the two apart either — it treats a 400 and a 413 on an upload identically, remembering the SHA so the same bytes are not re-sent — which is why the choice costs no compatibility today. The UTF-8 half is inert on this route (a JSON string decodes to valid UTF-8 by construction) and load-bearing on the sync path slice 4 adds, where the bytes come from a file; so is the check beside it that the content holds no U+0000 — legal UTF-8 that Postgres text refuses, already a 400 on every route through the body check above, and on the sync path a refusal remembered by digest rather than a settlement that fails the whole store on every run. *Evidence: "Maximum 100 kB (102,400 bytes)" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsMemory.Content and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryNewParams.Content); `case (isStatus(err, 400) || isStatus(err, 413)) && localSHA != ""` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go SessionMemoryStores.upload); internal/memsync/memsync.go ValidateContent, internal/api/errors.go errTypeRequestTooLarge (the 413 family this deliberately does not join); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryContentRules; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 9 — a create carrying 102,401 bytes of content: 400 or 413; the same recording should also send content that is not valid UTF-8, which item 9 does not yet name).*
- **The memories list — byte-wise path order, `path_prefix` defaulting to `/` and matched literally, and any `depth` but 0 or 1 refused (plan 36 slice 2, decision 5)** — Three readings in one place, because they are one endpoint's contract. **Order**: the reference promises "a stable, server-defined order" and says prefixes "interleave with `memory` items in path order" without saying which collation, so this platform sorts bytes, spelled `COLLATE "C"` on the column, its unique index, the `ORDER BY` and the cursor comparison — the executor already pins `LC_ALL=C` shell-side for the same reason, that `postgres:16-alpine` and Cloud SQL do not agree on `en_US.utf8`. **A 2026-09-02 recording refutes the premise**: the reference returned `/x/w.md`, `/x/y/z.md`, `/ä`, `/a`, `/A` — strict newest-first by `created_at`, not path order at all — so the collation question never arises there, and this platform's byte order is a mismatch rather than one reading of an underspecified promise. **#549** owns it. **`path_prefix`**: documented as "Must end with `/` (segment-aligned)", with no stated default and no stated answer for a value that does not; this platform defaults it to `/` (which every path starts with, so an omitted filter is no filter) and matches whatever arrives literally rather than refusing it — so `/notes/` cannot reach `/notes-archive/`, and a prefix of `/a_b` or `/100%` means those bytes, not a pattern. **The recording refutes this half too**: the reference answered a `path_prefix` of `/x`, without the trailing slash, with 400 "path_prefix must be segment-aligned and end with /", where this platform accepts it literally — also #549's. **`depth`**: documented as `0` (or omitted) and `1` with no bounds given; anything else is a 400 rather than a silent clamp, since a caller asking for `depth=2` wants something this endpoint cannot give. **This one the recording confirms**: `depth=2` answered 400, "depth must be 0 or 1 when provided". It adds one nuance that is ours to reconcile — with a `path_prefix` present the reference refuses `depth=0` too ("depth: must be greater than or equal to 1"), which this platform accepts. *Evidence: the list params' documentation of `path_prefix` and `depth` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.PathPrefix and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.Depth) and BetaManagedAgentsMemoryPrefix, "interleaves with `memory` items in path order" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryPrefix); internal/store/migrations/0029_memories.sql (the `COLLATE "C"` column), internal/api/memories.go listMemories, internal/executor/harvest.go (the LC_ALL=C precedent); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryList / TestMemoryListPrefixAndDepth; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 5* *Tracked: #549 (the recorded newest-first order, the refused non-segment-aligned prefix, and the `depth=0`-with-prefix refusal — all three recorded 2026-09-02; the `depth=2` bound is confirmed and needs no further recording, and the implied default prefix stays unprobed).*
- **A memory at `/.anthropic-memory-store`, or under it, is created like any other and never lands in a mount; a rename onto the path itself is refused with a 400 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4; #669)** — Every store directory a worker mounts carries a marker file of that name at its root, whose bytes are `"version 1\n<memory_store_id>"` and whose absence or alteration makes a worker stop trusting the directory. **The create half is recorded, and this platform now matches it**: the reference answers a create at that path with 200 and lists the memory as an ordinary row, applying no reserved-path rule at the API. This platform refused it with a 400 until #669 — an inference the recording refuted, as its documented path table (empty, `.` and `..` segments; no reserved name) already hinted. **Keeping such a memory off the marker is the consumers' job, and ours mirror the reference's own client**, whose worker skips a listed memory at that path with a warning rather than write it over the marker, and never uploads the marker. Both of ours do the same — the BYOC worker in its one listing loop, the executor where it reads a store's rows to land them and to settle a sync — so the memory is never written over the marker (which would leave the directory untrusted and every later write of the session withheld as pull-only), never pulled, and never recorded in a baseline, where its absence from the directory would read as a local deletion. It stays in the store and counts toward the 2,000-memory cap. **Ours skip a memory *under* that path too, and that step is ours alone**: the reference client's test is the exact path, and lets `/.anthropic-memory-store/x.md` through, yet such a memory can never land either — it needs a directory where the marker file is, so on a fresh mount the landing batch fails at the marker and on a landed one every batch that pulls it fails, taking the store's other pulls with it. The API accepts both a create and a rename at such a path, as it accepts any other: the reference's answer to either is unrecorded, and a refusal would be a divergence with nothing to argue it. **The rename half is our choice, unobserved**: no probe renamed a memory onto that path, and this platform keeps refusing one — 400 `invalid_request_error`, `path /.anthropic-memory-store is reserved for the memory store's marker file` — judged once the memory is read, so a missing memory answers its 404 first; an update naming the path the memory already holds is no rename and is not refused. Nothing else is involved: `/x/.anthropic-memory-store` is an ordinary memory that both workers sync, each excluding the marker by its root-relative path rather than its basename — the reference's by comparing root-relative keys, ours with `find . -type f ! -path './.anthropic-memory-store'`. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `mem.create.marker-root` and `mem.create.marker-nested` (both 200) and `mem.list.view=full` (both listed as ordinary rows); no probe renames onto the marker's path.* *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/self-hosted-sandboxes ("Each store directory contains a marker file named `.anthropic-memory-store`… the worker does not sync a directory whose marker is missing or altered"); `const MarkerPath = ".anthropic-memory-store"`, the skip on a listed memory at it, and the marker's exclusion from the upload (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go MarkerPath and SessionMemoryStores.listMemories and SessionMemoryStores.scanMarker); the spec's BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams.path, the documented rules, which name no reserved path (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams.properties.path); internal/memsync/memsync.go IsMarkerPath, ShadowsMarker and ValidatePath, internal/api/memories.go updateMemory, internal/executor/memory.go materializeStore and settleStore, internal/worker/memory.go listMemories, internal/memsync/tree.go HashTreeCommand, internal/sandbox/bulkwrite.go BulkPrepareShell and BulkRenameShell (the two refusals a memory under the marker meets); internal/memsync/memsync_test.go TestValidatePath / TestIsMarkerPath / TestShadowsMarker, internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryPathRules, internal/executor/memory_test.go and internal/worker/memory_test.go TestMemoryShadowingTheMarkerIsSkipped; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (a rename onto `/.anthropic-memory-store`, and a create or rename under it, none of which a recording has probed: the status of each); landed for #669.*
- **The memory-versions list defaults to 20 items per page (plan 36 slice 2, decision 5)** — Its `limit` parameter carries no documentation at all in the SDK ("Query parameter for limit") and no default in the guides, unlike its siblings: the memories list documents "Defaults to 20 when omitted", and so do the store and agent lists. Twenty is therefore taken from the siblings rather than observed, along with the 1–100 range this platform applies to every list. The ordering beside it is not an inference — the reference does state "ordered by `created_at` descending (newest first), with `id` as tiebreak". *Evidence: the list params, `limit` undocumented (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionListParams.Limit) against "Defaults to 20 when omitted" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.Limit); the spec's BetaManagedAgentsListMemoryVersionsResult, the documented order (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsListMemoryVersionsResult.properties.data); internal/api/memoryversions.go listMemoryVersions, internal/api/page.go defaultLimit; internal/api/memoryversions_test.go TestMemoryVersionList* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 8 — `GET …/memory_versions` with no `limit`: the page size it returns, and the shape of its `next_page`).*
- **A memory-update precondition without its `content_sha256` is a 400 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The precondition object's schema requires only `type`; `content_sha256` is optional in both the spec and the SDK (`param.Opt`), and what the reference does with `{"precondition": {"type": "content_sha256"}}` is unobserved. This platform refuses it with a 400 rather than treating a precondition with nothing to compare against as an unconditional write — the silent alternative is the one a sync client would never notice. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsContentSha256Precondition`, `required: ["type"]` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsContentSha256Precondition.required); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsPreconditionParam; internal/api/memories.go updateMemory; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryUpdateSemantics ("a bodyless precondition")* *Tracked: #78 (not among the plan's fifteen items: an update sent with a type-only precondition would settle it).*
- **The 2,001st memory is refused with a 400 `invalid_request_error` (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The memory guide says a store holds at most 2,000 memories and that "writes to new memories fail" past it, and stops there: no status, no error type. This platform answers 400 `memory store … holds 2000 memories`, counted under the store row's lock so two concurrent creates cannot both squeeze in. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the 2,000 limit); internal/api/memories.go createMemory (`memoriesPerStore`); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryStoreCapacity; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (not among the plan's fifteen items: a 2,001st create against the reference would settle the status and type).*
- **Memory create and update — an explicit JSON null on `content` or `path` (plan 36 slice 2)** — The create schema lists `content` as required yet types it `anyOf [string, null]`; the update schema admits null on both `content` and `path` and gives it no meaning, and the SDK never sends one. This platform reads them by the rule the memory-store update already registered one entry up: on create a null `content` is a 400 (`content is required`), on update a null `content` or `path` is the omitted case — so a body of nulls is the documented "neither field" 400. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams` and `BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams; BetaMemoryStoreMemoryNewParams and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryUpdateParams, the latter with `param.Opt` fields (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryNewParams and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryUpdateParams); internal/api/memories.go createMemory / updateMemory; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryContentRules / TestMemoryUpdateSemantics* *Tracked: #78 (not among the plan's fifteen items: the three null forms sent to the reference would settle them).*
- **The memory element has no id, and `GET`/`DELETE …/resources/{rid}` answer 404 for it (plan 36 slice 3, decision 7)** — The response variant carries `memory_store_id, type, access, description, instructions, mount_path, name` and nothing else (`instructions` only when the attachment supplied one, the recorded shape the element entry among the divergences above describes) — no `id`, `created_at` or `updated_at`, where the file and repository variants carry all three — so nothing in the element can serve as a `{rid}`. Yet the get and update response unions of `…/resources/{rid}` list the memory variant, so the reference has some handle that returns one; which is unrecorded. This platform's get and delete admit only `sesrsc_` ids, so a `memory_store_id` in the path is the shape-check 404 like any other non-`sesrsc_` value, and the attachment can be neither read alone nor removed: attach is create-time only (the SDK's add endpoint is typed file-only), so the session's lifetime is the attachment's. The resources list serves the element in place and pages across it by a key of its own (`memstore:<memory_store_id>`, unique per session by the same-store-twice rule) inside the cursor, which stays opaque. *Evidence: the three variants' key sets, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStoreResource and BetaManagedAgentsFileResource and BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResource; the unions carrying the memory variant, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaSessionResourceGetResponseUnion and BetaSessionResourceUpdateResponseUnion; add is file-only, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaSessionResourceAddParams and BetaSessionResourceAddParams.MarshalJSON; internal/api/sessionresources.go checkResourceID / resourceKey; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachment / TestSessionResourceListPagesAcrossMemoryElements* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 3 — `GET`/`DELETE …/resources/{rid}` with the `memory_store_id` and with a `sesrsc_` id: which handle, if any, returns the memory element).*
- **Stores whose slugs collide all attach, each later one suffixed `-2`, `-3`, … — recorded for a pair, ours beyond it (plan 36 slice 3, decision 8)** — Nothing published says what two stores that slug alike — "notes" and "NOTES", one mount path — do in one session. The 2026-09-02 recording answers it for a pair, twice: `session.create.case-colliding-stores` ("notes", "NOTES") and `session.create.five-stores` ("notes", then "(Notes)") each answered 200, the earlier store at `/mnt/memory/notes` and the later at `/mnt/memory/notes-2`. This platform refused such a create with a 400 naming both stores — an unregistered divergence, and one that failed every fire of a deployment carrying such a pair — and now attaches both the same way. The rest of the rule is ours, because a pair cannot show it. "Earlier" is request order: in both probes the earlier store was also the earlier created, so creation order fits the recording as well, while the store ids' lexical order does not (the five-stores pair runs against it). Every store claims its own slug before any suffix is handed out, so a suffix never displaces a store whose name slugs to it: beside "notes" and "NOTES", a store named "notes 2" keeps `notes-2` and "NOTES" takes `notes-3`, in whichever order the three are attached. The stores that lost then take the first of `-2`, `-3`, … that nothing has claimed. A suffixed slug is held to 255 bytes, the Linux NAME_MAX, since a longer mount directory would fail every write under it — a 255-character name slugs to 255 bytes, so the slug is cut short enough for its suffix, a hyphen the cut leaves at the end dropped, and the cut candidate is checked against the claimed slugs like any other; no unsuffixed slug needs the cut, a name being at most 255 characters that each slug to at most one byte and the id fallback 33 bytes. And the whole-id fallback is a slug like any other, so a store named after an all-symbol store's id collides with it. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.case-colliding-stores` and `session.create.five-stores` in `batch2.json`; the stores' creation order, `store.create.notes` and `store.create.(Notes)` in `free_batch1.json`, `store.create.NOTES` in `batch2.json`); internal/api/sessionresources.go suffixCollidingMemoryMounts and memoryMountNameMax; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreSlugCollisions; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 8* *Tracked: #78 (three stores that slug alike, a store named "notes 2" beside two named "notes", a colliding pair attached against their creation order, and two 255-character names that slug alike — recording item 7 took only the pair); landed for #671.*
- **The same store attached twice, and a ninth store, are each a 400 (plan 36 slice 3, decision 7)** — The cap is documented ("8 stores per session"); what exceeding it answers is not, nor what two attachments of one store answer. The second is recorded: `session.create.same-store-twice` answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "resources contains duplicate memory_store_id: …", which this platform matches on status and type, the message (`memory store … is attached more than once`) its own. The ninth is unrecorded and refused the same way here. Both are judged before any row is read. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.same-store-twice`); platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the per-session limit); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseResourceInputs; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachmentRejections; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 7* *Tracked: #78 (a ninth store — not among the plan's fifteen items, and would need a call of its own).*
- **Attaching a well-formed memory store id that names no store is a 404 `not_found_error` (#668)** — The spec leaves an unknown id's status unstated. The entry this replaces answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, on the `vault_ids` precedent; this platform now answers 404 `not_found_error`, `memory store … not found` — the words its own `GET /v1/memory_stores/{id}` already used. What the reference does here is inferred rather than recorded. Its one recorded attach of an unknown store, `session.create.unknown-store`, did answer **404 `not_found_error`** ("Memory store `memstore_…` not found."), but that id's token is 25 characters where every real one is 24, so it cannot tell "absent" from "malformed". The well-formed case rests on siblings: a 24-character absent id answered 404 `not_found_error` on `GET /v1/memory_stores/{id}` and, as an `environment_id`, on session create itself (2026-09-05), and a deleted file mounted at session create answered 404 (2026-09-02) — so an id naming nothing is a 404 on every resource and route the recordings reach, while a row that exists but is archived is a 400 (the archived-store entry among the divergences above). A deployment fire still records the vanished store as `session_resource_not_found_error` (its own entry below): the 404 is wrapped with that type, so a manual run settles it on a 200 and a scheduled fire pauses on it. Dream create applies the same rule to its input store (the dream-create entry below). The `vault_ids` entry this one borrowed from stays an inference with its 400: no unknown vault was recorded. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.unknown-store`, `sessF2.create.deleted-file-mount`) and the 2026-09-05 recording (`item1.read.absent-store-control`, `item1.create.session-absent-env`); internal/api/sessionresources.go snapshotMemoryStore; internal/api/memorystores.go; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachmentRejections / TestSessionMemoryAttachLocksTheStoreRow; internal/api/deploymentscheduler_test.go TestDeletedMemoryStoreFailsAFireAsClassified* *Tracked: #78 (a session create attaching a well-formed, absent memory store id — the one recorded attach used a malformed id, so the 404 rests on its siblings); landed for #668.*
- **A deleted store's attachment is kept as snapshotted (plan 36 slice 3, decision 7)** — Deleting a store cascades its memories and versions and leaves every session's element in place: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` still renders it and the `memory_store_id` filter still matches, exactly as a deleted file's mount stays in `resources` (plan 08 decision 2's accepted dangling reference). What the reference renders for such a session, and what its next run tells the model, are unrecorded; since slice 4 the brain's block renders the element hedged — "NOT AVAILABLE: the memory store no longer exists" — and the executor materializes nothing for it, counting the miss. *Evidence: internal/store/migrations/0028_memory_stores.sql and 0029_memories.sql (the cascades end at the store); internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachment (the element after the store's delete) / TestSessionListFiltersByMemoryStore; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 7* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 12 — a session whose store is deleted after attach: the session GET's element, and the next run's prompt if one is observable).*
- **The Memory-stores block — wording and placement inferred (plan 36 slice 4, decision 9)** — A session's system prompt gains a "Memory stores" block after the Mounted-repositories block and before any runtime `system.message` text: a lead line telling the model the directories persist through the store and that a read-only one takes no writes, then one `<mount_path> — <name> (<access>)` bullet per attached store, with the snapshotted description and instructions when present, `(read_only, archived)` for a store archived since the attachment, and a NOT AVAILABLE hedge for one deleted since. The block renders on `self_hosted` as on `cloud` since slice 6, both sandboxes now landing the store. The reference documents that a store's `instructions` reach the agent and that the mount path is told to it; the wording, the placement and the per-store facts it states are inferences, as the files and repositories blocks' are. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (`instructions` "guide the agent's use of the store"); internal/brain/memory.go (resolveMemoryBlock, renderMemoryBlock); internal/brain/replay.go (buildRequest placement); internal/brain/memoryinject_test.go* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 1 — a session with two stores, one `read_only` with `instructions`, asked to print every part of its system prompt that mentions `/mnt/memory`).*
- **Cron day-of-month and day-of-week — the POSIX union reading is recorded as wrong; the frequency floor is still unprobed (plan 37 slice 1)** — The published dialect fixes the grammar precisely — five fields, DOW 0-7 with both 0 and 7 Sunday, no seconds or year field, no L / W / # / ?, no @daily — but says nothing about the classic POSIX rule that a restricted day-of-month and a restricted day-of-week are OR'd rather than AND'd, and the strongest evidence for the union was the dialect's own name: both schemas call it a "5-field POSIX cron schedule", and union is what POSIX specifies. **A recording refuted it** (2026-09-02): the reference does not union the two fields, it refuses the expression outright — `0 0 1 * 1` answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "`schedule.expression` cannot restrict both day-of-month and day-of-week; use `*` for one" — so this platform accepts at create what the reference rejects, and then fires on the union. **That is a bug, not a divergence**, so it has no entry of its own: #543 owns it, with the recorded message, and a reviewer must not clear it on this entry's authority — note that `internal/cron/cron_test.go` TestDayOfMonthUnionDayOfWeek currently pins the very behavior #543 will change. What stays inferred is the frequency floor: none is published, so an expression matching every minute is accepted here, and no probe covered it. The reference's other two create-time schedule refusals do match this platform, and are recorded rather than inferred now — an unsatisfiable expression is 400 on both sides ("does not produce any occurrences in the next year" there, `deploymentparse.go`'s empty-`Upcoming` check here), as is a 6-field expression. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`deployment.create.dom+dow-cron`, `deployment.create.unsatisfiable-cron`, `deployment.create.6-field-cron`); the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule` and `BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams` (both descriptions open "5-field POSIX cron schedule"; their expression descriptions carry the dialect verbatim and identically), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams; the same sentence, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsSchedule.Expression; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — "Maximum granularity supported is at the minute level"); internal/cron/cron.go matchesDay, internal/api/deploymentparse.go, internal/cron/cron_test.go TestDayOfMonthUnionDayOfWeek.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference enforces any floor on schedule frequency stays unprobed — create a deployment that fires every minute and read upcoming_runs_at; #543 owns the recorded day-of-month/day-of-week refusal).*
- **`upcoming_runs_at` returns five occurrences whenever five exist (plan 37 slice 1)** — The schema says "up to 5" and publishes when the array is non-empty and when it is empty; it does not say when fewer than five come back for a live schedule. We return five whenever five exist within a twelve-year search bound — twelve rather than a round four because a leap-day expression has an eight-year gap across the non-leap year 2100, and a shorter bound would return an empty array for a live schedule the same schema requires to be non-empty. An expression with no occurrence inside that bound is refused at create and update rather than stored, which is the entry below. The two behaviors the schema does state — non-empty for active and paused deployments, empty once archived_at is set — are implemented as published and are not inferences. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.upcoming_runs_at`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.properties.upcoming_runs_at; the same sentence, and no api: tag, making the key optional, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsSchedule.UpcomingRunsAt; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-29 — its create example answers a weekly schedule with three occurrences, not five, which is an example rather than a rule but is the closest thing to an observation of the reference's count); internal/api/deployments.go fillScheduleTimestamps, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentScheduleComputesItsTimestamps.* *Tracked: #78 (compare a weekly and a per-minute schedule's arrays on one recording).*
- **A cron expression with no occurrence is refused at create and update (plan 37 slice 1)** — An expression naming 31 February parses, every field is in range, and that date never arrives; the reference publishes no validation rule for such an expression, only that `upcoming_runs_at` is non-empty for an active deployment — which is unsatisfiable together. We answer 400 rather than storing a schedule that can never fire, searching the same twelve-year bound `upcoming_runs_at` uses. Twelve years rather than four because a leap-day expression has an eight-year gap across 2100, which a shorter bound would misreport as unsatisfiable. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams.expression` (the dialect, with no validation rule), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams.properties.expression, and `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.upcoming_runs_at` ("non-empty for active and paused deployments"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.properties.upcoming_runs_at; internal/cron/cron.go SearchYears, internal/api/deploymentparse.go parseDeploymentSchedule, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentScheduleRejections.* *Tracked: #78 (post an unsatisfiable expression to the reference and read the status code).*
- **Update, pause and unpause on an archived deployment are 400s (plan 37 slice 1)** — Archive is terminal and the reference publishes no status code for acting on an archived deployment through any path; the spec attaches no operation-specific errors to any deployment endpoint. We answer 400 "deployment ... is archived" on update, pause and unpause alike, matching how this platform already refuses to act on an archived resource, while GET and the list with include_archived still return the row. Leaving update unspecified would leave hidden configuration mutable on a row nothing can ever fire. The manual-run path takes the same refusal one slice later. *Evidence: the spec's deployment update, pause and unpause operations, no per-endpoint error semantics on any of the three, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Update and BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, every error status answering the one shared error body, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaErrorResponse; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — archive described as terminal, with no status code for acting on one); internal/api/deployments.go loadDeployment, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestArchiveDeploymentIsTerminalForMutation.* *Tracked: #78 (update, pause and unpause an archived deployment on the reference, and read the status codes).*
- **A deployment `description` set to the empty string reads back as null (plan 37 slice 1)** — The field is in the Deployment object's required list and typed string-or-null, so null is the reference's own spelling of unset; the create params accept a string with no minimum length, so the empty string is legal input. This platform stores the column NOT NULL with an empty-string default and renders the empty string as null, which round-trips every value except one: a caller who sets description to "" reads back null. The two are not distinguishable in storage and are not distinguishable in meaning, and the alternative — a second nullable column added by a later migration to preserve a distinction nothing acts on — buys nothing. What the reference does with an explicit empty string is unrecorded. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsDeployment.description` (required, string-or-null), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeployment, and `BetaManagedAgentsCreateDeploymentParams.description` (no minLength), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateDeploymentParams.properties.description; internal/store/migrations/0031_deployments.sql (the column), internal/api/deployments.go scanDeployment.* *Tracked: #78 (create a deployment with description "" and read it back).*
- **A deployment echoes a repository's resolved `mount_path`, not the one it was given (plan 37 slice 1)** — The resource config "echoes the input minus write-only credentials", and `mount_path` is optional with a documented default of a workspace directory named for the repository. This platform resolves the default when the input omits it and echoes the resolved path, so a create that sent no `mount_path` reads one back. The resolved value is what a session created from the deployment will actually mount, which is the question a reader of the echo is asking; whether the reference echoes the given value or the resolved one is not decidable from the types, since both satisfy the schema. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResourceConfig.mount_path` (nullable, with the documented default), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResourceConfig.properties.mount_path, and `BetaManagedAgentsSessionResourceConfig` ("echoes the input"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsSessionResourceConfig; internal/api/sessionresources.go defaultRepoMountRoot, internal/api/deploymentresources.go deploymentResourcesFrom.* *Tracked: #78 (create a deployment with a repository and no mount_path, and read the resources echo).*
- **Pause and unpause are idempotent 200s, unpause does not re-validate the cause, and a body on an action endpoint is ignored (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference documents neither a conflict for pausing an already-paused deployment nor a precondition failure for unpausing an active one, says nothing about whether unpausing an error-paused deployment is refused while the cause persists, and declares no requestBody on any of the four action endpoints. We answer 200 in all four idempotency cases, returning the resource; unpause clears paused_at and both reason columns unconditionally, and the next scheduled fire re-discovers the cause and re-pauses if it is still there; a request body is neither read nor refused. *Evidence: the spec's deployment pause and unpause operations, neither declaring a request body, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, and neither carrying per-endpoint error semantics, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaErrorResponse; the four action params structs, which carry only headers, the beta and workspace ones, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentArchiveParams and BetaDeploymentPauseParams and BetaDeploymentRunParams and BetaDeploymentUnpauseParams; internal/api/deployments.go setDeploymentPause, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentPauseAndUnpause.* *Tracked: #78 (six calls: pause twice, unpause twice, each against an archived deployment, and one with a non-empty body).*
- **A deployment's initial_events must satisfy the same adjacency rule a posted batch does (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference's deployment initial_events union admits user.message, user.define_outcome and system.message with no stated ordering constraint. This platform routes a deployment's list through events.NormalizeInbound, the same normalizer POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events uses, which requires a system.message to be the final event of the request and to immediately follow a user.message, user.tool_result or user.custom_tool_result. So a user.message followed by a system.message is accepted and a lone system.message is a 400 — narrower than the union states. Calling the one normalizer at every entry point beats a second copy of the rules whose ordering could drift from the first, and refusing at create beats recording a failed run every night on a list that can never normalize. *Evidence: the spec's BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentInitialEventParams (the three-type discriminated union, with no ordering constraint), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentInitialEventParams; internal/events/inbound.go NormalizeInbound (the adjacency rule), internal/api/deploymentwire.go validateDeploymentInitialEvents, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentInitialEventsMustSurviveTheNormalizer.* *Tracked: #78 (post a deployment whose initial_events is a lone system.message, and record the status).*
- **A `user.message` whose `content` is a plain string is accepted, and echoed back as one (plan 35 slice 0, surfaced on a typed object by plan 37 slice 1)** — Every reference schema types this content as an array of blocks — `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.content` and `BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.content` alike — and the SDK's `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParamsContentUnion` has arms for text, image, document and redacted blocks and none for a bare string. This platform accepts the string form on inbound `user.message` and stores it verbatim, so the event echoes back as a string. Replay renders it as a single text block, which is why the empty string is refused there like an empty text block. The leniency long predates deployments and is left standing rather than tightened, because narrowing it would break session clients already sending it; what plan 37 changes is the blast radius, since a deployment's `initial_events` are echoed inside a schema'd response object, where an SDK client decoding the array type sees no content at all. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.content` and `BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.content` (both `type: array`), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.properties.content and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.properties.content; four block arms, no string arm, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParamsContentUnion; internal/events/inbound.go normalizeUserMessage.* *Tracked: #78 (post a string-content user.message to the reference and record the status).*
- **The deployments list refuses `status` beside `include_archived` on presence, not on value (plan 37 slice 1)** — The published sentence is "To include archived deployments, use `include_archived` instead; the two cannot be combined", which settles the contradictory pair and leaves the redundant one open: `?status=active&include_archived=false` asks for something coherent, since excluding archived rows is what the status filter already does. We answer 400 to both, keying the refusal on the parameter being sent rather than on how its boolean parses — "cannot be combined" reads as a rule about sending both, and a caller who sends both has a belief about the endpoint worth correcting once rather than silently satisfying. The blast radius is small: the `ant` CLI sends only flags the user set, so `--status active` alone is unaffected and only an explicit `--include-archived=false` beside it is refused. *Evidence: the quoted sentence, with no statement about the redundant pair and the same text as the list operation's parameter description, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentListParams.Status; unset flags omitted from the request, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — requestflag.go ExtractRequestContents; internal/api/deployments.go listDeployments, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestListDeployments.* *Tracked: #78 (send status with include_archived=false to the reference and read the status code).*
